# Mercury notes (stop agent memory)

Read `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md` first, then this file.

## Where things stand

- Branch `agent/mercury` (code, JSON, notes). Looks go to `agent/mercury-looks` through `npm run looks -- --push --folder mercury --note "..."` only, at least every 30 minutes.
- Parent is `/root` (the Builder). Milestone messages: orbit view, terrain view, dive and rise, then `READY`. Each with commit, measured numbers, weak points. Do not wait for replies.
- Builder's notes: web set at most 35 MB on disk, full 150 MB. Engine requests early as `assets-src/mercury/engine.patch` (request 1, `terrain.rollDeg`, is on main). Travels before the hold and after the rise must stay free of pops (judged in Phase 5).
- VM: 4 cores, about 8 GB RAM. Two heavy Python jobs at once get killed: run one at a time. One render process at a time.

## Segments and judged frames (30 fps)

- hold 18 to 23 s, judged frame 615 (holdMid 20.5 s). dive 23 to 26 s, judged 717. terrain 26 to 32 s, judged 870. rise 32 to 34.5 s, judged 1016. Covers centered on frames 744 and 990. The Sun stop is before (travel 15 to 18 s), Venus after.

## How the orbit view is built (state at 03:45 UTC Nov 21)

- `globe.ts`: stop-local impostor. A back-side shell of radius 1.3, ray-sphere in the fragment shader, analytic limb coverage (clean at every tier, no haze, no glow at the limb from air). Shade = (curve(ndl) - night) x limb(mu) x relief lift + night. `curve.json` holds both tables, measured from the hero by `wrap/curve.py`: the hero brightens up to 2.46 times toward the lit limb (mu below 0.5), separate from the ndl curve.
- Lens glow outside the lit limb is a film layer in the same shader (the sky slides behind the globe in the hold, so it cannot live in the sky patch). It follows curve(dot(limb normal, Sun-in-sky direction projected on the image plane)), gated by the light on the limb. Hero glow is centered on position angle about 68 degrees (toward the Sun in frame), not on the light vector (57 degrees).
- `sunplate.ts`: the Sun is stop-local. The engine Sun cannot match the hero (flat pale disc, core dot, wide glow), so `sunGlare` is 0.012 and a sky-fixed additive plate draws the hero Sun (hero pixels plus two outpaints to complete the disc), fixed to the hold camera at holdMid, breathing with a sine that is zero at holdMid. In the arriving travel it fades in with smooth(ramp(0.25, 0.8, u)), the same ramp the engine uses for the Sun's glare; in the leaving travel it fades out with the same ramp. The plate carries no stars (polar low-percentile smoothing past 1.2 Sun radii).
- Sky patch `sky-hold.webp` (3520x1980 full, half for web, gain 4 with skyExposure 0.25): hero sky with the planet removed, minus the Sun plate, minus the film's bloom (frame 615 rendered with the rim glow off and a black sky patch). Hero stars kept between 1.012 and 1.3 radii on a star-free filled background, so no rim glow is baked in the sky.
- Wrap v1: `wrap/guide.py` (real mosaic graded to the hero's albedo statistics) then `wrap/match.py` (hero-2x pixels divided by the fitted shade, faded out between view cosine 0.42 and 0.2, tone ratio carried to the limb). The far side is still the unpainted guide.

## Measured (frame 615, review tier, hero vs film, scene luminance)

- Disc: night side ratio 0.96 to 1.10 by radius, day side 0.98 to 1.01 by ndl band.
- Sky: low-pass luminance ratio film/hero median 1.018 (10th to 90th percentile 0.969 to 1.057).
- Hero Sun: center (2690.65, 175.88) hero px, radius 564 px (9.4 degrees).

## Session 2 (03:47 to 04:15 UTC): what changed

- Registration: the Sun plate and the sky patch are fixed to `plan.hold(holdMid - hold.start).quaternion x drift(plan.holdMid)` (`drift` from `director/rig`). The film's disc sits (2.11, -1.41) px at 1080p off the hero's from the engine's camera drift; accepted.
- Lens ghost in `near` (`lens.ts`, `look.lens`). It was about (-7.3, -5.7) px at 1080p from the hero's: nudge left.
- Night side: the map's night side is the painted guide times a scalar level (0.88), not hero pixels (the hero night side is smooth and carries lens artifacts). Shader: `col = (ground * lit + mix(vec3(nightFlat), ground, nightDetail) * night * (1 + nightVeil * smoothstep(nightVeilFrom, 1, mu))) * tint`. Night ratio film/hero by radius 1.05, 1.07, 1.00, 1.07, 1.07, 1.01; day by ndl band 1.00 to 1.04; rim glow outside the limb at 1.006 to 1.012 radii 0.77 to 0.93 (a little low), good farther out.
- Guide v3 (`wrap/guide.py`): color and low-frequency albedo from the real color mosaic (blur 7 px at 4096, graded to hero statistics, polar fade 58 to 74 degrees), crater structure from the real DEM at 8192 with light baked for the film's Sun direction (the hero carries its relief the same way), `MARK 0.22`, `STEEP 2.5`, `SOFT 0.05`, `UNIT 0.5`, `LOW 0.6`. v1 (mosaic only) kept the seams and blocks of the mosaic; v2 (non-directional marks only) gave blobby "lichen" takes.
- Tiles (`wrap/tiles.py cut|build`): 38 gnomonic tiles of 2048 px (e0 to e9, n0 to n7, s0 to s7, np0 to np4, sp0 to sp4, np, sp). Each is repainted by the image model with `style.jpg` (hero day region) as the style reference, prompt `wrap/prompts/tile.txt` (`tile-pole.txt` for np, sp), checked with `wrap/flowcheck.py`, low frequencies locked to the input at sigma 24 px, splat back. Takes so far: `a1` nano on all 38 (old guide), `b1` seedream on e7, n6, np, e2, `t1`/`t2` old; round on guide v3: `c1` seedream and `d1` nano on all 38 (running at 04:11).
- Height map (`wrap/height.py`): real DEM at 4096x2048, flattened to its 40 px low-pass where the hero day pixels are kept (the hero carries its own shading). `wrap-height.bin` uint16, `assets.ts` `wrap.height: true`, shader relief by normal perturbation, mild.
- Merged `origin/main` b4ae65c (engine hash 434d0a5e367a62fd): stills before it are stale. Shader tip from the Builder: expensive conditional work goes in `for (int k = 0; k < (cond ? 1 : 0); k++)` with `textureGrad`/`textureLod`.
- Terrain inputs: `out/work/mercury/close-2x.png` passes `checkmoved` (p99 under 1.5 px). Depth: `out/work/mercury/close-depth.raw` (float32 2816x1584, near = 1). Camera fit `relief/camfit.py` -> `relief/camera-fit.json`: vfov 60 assumed, pitch down -30.05 (camera looks down 30 degrees... sign as Mars: see file), roll -11.27 degrees, dip 10.25 degrees, planet radius 61.7 camera heights, horizon distance 11.15 heights, rms 0.022 degrees.

Local patch dropped, main merged (aa3e4fb). `engine.patch` and `ENGINE-REQUESTS.md` stay as the record. Check script: `npx tsx out/work/mercury/check-roll.ts <rollDeg> <altitudeScale>`. - Glare gate (`look.light.glareGate [0.616, 0.766, 0.35]`): limb brightening and rim glow are lens glare, so they only apply where the drawn Sun is near the view ray. Frame 615 numbers did not change.

Seedream adds real fine texture and crisp rims; nano keeps the blur and draws embossed white lines, so seedream is used. All kept takes pass the flow check (median under 0.4 px, p99 under 1 px at 2048). `wrap/takes.json` lists the take per tile.
- Terrain margins (`relief/ext.py`): canvas 4096 x 1932 at approved scale (640 px left and right, 348 px below, nothing above). Left and right squares outpainted with nano (take t1 kept; seedream t2 on the left changed the whole picture to orange streaks, rejected), then the gap below with nano (t1 kept, t2 had a visible rectangle). Approved pixels are pasted back exactly. Painted horizon follows the fitted sphere within 1 to 4 px, except the far right edge (5 to 12 px low): the alpha cut uses the detected horizon in the margins.
- Relief build (`relief/geom.py`, `relief/build.py`): distance = sphere ground model x exp(detail), detail = high-pass (300 px) of log(depth fit) minus log(sphere), gain 1.6, faded out between 4.5 and 8 camera heights so the horizon stays a clean curve. One layer, alpha cut at the horizon, black masks.
- The `read` tool caches pictures by name: write each picture to a new file name.

## Terrain plan

- Engine class `Relief.load(ctx, base, def, motion)` in a `terrain` group as `src/stops/mars/set.ts`; `ReliefDef` {imageSize, vfovDeg, layers [{color, alpha?, dist, box, distSize, tint}], masks, maskBox, mistDir}; dist files raw float32 at half the layer size; masks black (no mist, no dust on Mercury).
- Distance = fit of 1 / (a x depth^g + b) to a sphere ground model from the camera fit. One layer first; a cut layer only if a sweep shows stretching. Margins outpainted about 12 percent left, right, bottom. Sky cut by alpha at the horizon.
- Terrain sky patch from the close image's sky (stars, Sun bloom at top left near x 730, y 10 of 2816x1584, glow along the horizon). `skyAim: { terrain: [...] }` in `plan.ts`.

## Decisions

- Terrain: spot near the terminator, light about 8 degrees above the local horizon, heading = light azimuth turned 26 degrees right, horizon tilt about 10.7 degrees, dip about 6.8 degrees at vfov 60. The terrain Sun comes from the terrain sky patch.
- Cover: engine cover as a warm flash plus stop-local glare streaks in `near`.
- Mars open items to avoid: covers that read as a cut, stepped disc edge, razor night limb, smeared far side, blown areas, no soft terminator in the rise, soft shadowed foreground, hard horizon glow edge, repeated patterns.

## Engine facts that matter here

- Render order: sky, `world`, `near` (depth cleared), dust, cover. Camera at the origin; the world group sits at origin minus camera with `state.rotation`.
- Sky patches: replace mode, coords = aim^-1 x world ray, orbit aim = frame rotation x hold camera at frame 615. Patch weight 1 in hold, dive, terrain; 0 for the orbit patch in the rise; arriving travel smoothstep(u, 0.2, 0.8); leaving travel 0.
- Travel from the Sun (index 0): the Sun set fades out and the engine Sun fades in over u 0.3 to 0.42 at Mercury's `sunRadiusDeg`; glare mixes to Mercury's value over u 0.25 to 0.8.
- `plan.sunDir` (where the Sun is drawn) comes from `fit.sunInFrame`; `plan.lightDir` shades the globe. They are 62 degrees apart in the hero.
- Post: bloom 6 levels, filmic, vignette, grain, sharpen. Stills: `npm run still -- --frame N --tier T --out FILE [--diag nograin]`.

## Decisions (session 5)

- Cover: a stop-local full-screen glare in `near`, additive, alpha factors zero/one, centred on the Sun's screen position (orbit side: the drawn Sun; terrain side: the approved Sun at (745, -40) px of 2816x1584), white for about frames 741 to 747 and 987 to 993, thinning over ten frames or more on each side, with streaks. The engine cover is hidden inside it (`coverEdge: [1, 1]`, cover color far above white). No engine request.

## Session 6 (05:40 UTC on): what changed

- Spot moved onto the hero's ring crater: `plan.spot {x 0.5144, y 0.65436}`, lon 14.47205059, lat -13.87220011, light elevation 5.018 degrees, heading [0.967, 0.1376, -0.2145]. The ring crater (about 2.0 by 2.7 degrees) is the terraced crater of the terrain view; in the 19 degree patch it sits at about (0.59, 0.535), in the 9.5 degree patch at about (0.67, 0.57). North-up patches: Sun to the upper right (53 degrees clockwise from north), shadows to the lower left.
- Cover is the stop's own glare (`src/stops/mercury/glare.ts`, `look.glare`), drawn in `near`, additive, no alpha. A bloom centred on the Sun whose radius grows until it fills the frame (`floor`: amplitude `peak * steep(s, curve)`, radius `reach * exp(-shrink * (1 - s))`, profile `1 / (1 + (r / R)^2)^2` plus a small even veil) and a Sun term with rays. `s` is 1 inside `plateau` frames of the cover centre and falls over `rampOrbit` or `rampTerrain` frames. The engine cover is made invisible with `coverEdge: [4, 4]` (amount peaks at 0.043, alpha stays zero) and `cover: [1, 0.95, 0.86]`. The view switches at frames 744 and 990; frames 742 and 746 must be pure white (the tone curve needs a scene value of 8 or more).
- Sun on screen (ndc x, y): 734 (1.04, 0.58), 738 (0.77, 0.54), 740 (0.46, 0.39), 741 (0.21, 0.14), 742 (-0.01, -0.30); terrain 744 to 989 about (-0.47, 1.0); rise 990 (0.02, -0.48), 994 (0.08, -0.08), 996 (0.08, 0.30), 998 (0.02, 0.80), 1000 (-0.10, 1.36). - Terrain-side glare bug (06:10): the glare's Sun on the terrain side was taken from the sky patch aim as if it were a camera frame, which put it behind the camera, so every strip before `g6` showed flat fog there. Wrap texels per pixel at the view centre, 1080p (`npx tsx out/work/mercury/tex-per-px.ts`, `FADE=a,b`, `H=2160` for uhd): hold 2.06 to 2.12, 710 1.78, 717 1.64, 722 1.50, 726 1.33, 730 1.09, 734 0.81; rise 1004 1.08, 1007 1.47, 1010 1.88, 1016 2.43. With `fade [1.02, 1.5]` level 1 comes in over frames 723 to 731 (8 frames) and leaves over 1004 to 1007 (3 frames).

Level 1 weight at the view centre: 710 0.09, 717 0.26, 722 0.48, 726 0.74, 730 0.98 (before: 724 0.07, 726 0.28, 728 0.62, 730 0.93); rise 1004 1.0, 1007 0.52, 1010 0.02. The gold rims at the terminator now build over about 25 frames instead of 8 (`out/work/mercury/h1/native-a.png`), and rise 1007 is crisper (`h1/n1007-a.png`).

- Reversing the spin so the baked band turns into the night is not allowed: `kit/02-STOPS.md` "Every globe turns during its `hold`, slowly, in its real direction", positive `spinDegPerSecond` is prograde and Mercury is prograde.
- The band beyond the hero's terminator (09:10 to 09:25). The spin (0.6 degrees per second, zero at 615) turns the dawn terminator into the night side of the map: 2.0 degrees at 717, 2.6 at 744, 7.5 at 1004, 7.8 at 1016, 8.4 at 1034. That band (Sun cosine at 615 from 0 down to about -0.15) is the painted map, and it is poor: grey, smooth, embossed like clay with black hook shadows, no fine grain (`out/work/mercury/h1/band-painted-wrap-a.png`: painted map top, wrap bottom; `h2/patch1-mid-a.jpg`: level 1 has a smooth featureless band there). The hero's edge beside it carries teal and purple chroma blotches (hero noise divided by a tiny shade). It shows as lumps at the lower left of dive 722 to 736 and as a soft terminator in rise 1004 to 1034.
- Spin rest (09:22 to 09:33, kept). The globe's spin now rests while the film is in the terrain view: `set.ts` draws the globe with `plan.spin(t) - rate * look.spinRest * clamp(t - diveCover, 0, riseCover - diveCover)` (the two cover centres, frames 744 and 990, 8.2 s, 4.92 degrees). Closed form in film time, no change before the dive cover, same rate again in the rise and the travel. The terrain view shows a fixed Sun, so the planet resting under it is also what the terrain view says.
  - Why: in the rise the globe had turned 7.5 to 8.4 degrees since frame 615, which put the grey clay band of the painted map at the terminator. With the rest it has turned 2.6 to 3.5 degrees, so the terminator of the rise sits on the hero's own crisp ground with its gold rims (`out/work/mercury/h3/pair1013-a.png`, `h4/pair1016-a.png`, `h4/full1016-a.jpg`: before left, after right).
  - The engine's camera still follows the unrested turn (`plan.turn`), so in the rise the camera comes up 4.92 degrees dayward of the spot on the drawn globe. Sun and camera are fixed in the world, so the lit shape on screen is exactly as before; only the map slides under it. Frames 990 to 1002 are under the glare.
  - Checks: draft 726 byte identical to the render before (24430175); 870 final ae7f9d8a byte identical; G13 every third frame PASS, 0 of 155 over 2 percent, worst 998 0.11 percent, 1001 0.03 (before 0.18, 0.01); 1007 review rungs along 1.41, 1.34, 0.47, across 4.26, 4.36, 1.54. 1016 review: laplacian rms on the globe 0.1234 against 0.1327 before, tiles at the terminator 0.74 to 0.94: the clay band's black outlines are gone from the frame, the picture is the hero's ground (judged by eye at 3x, not by this number).
  - `look.spinRest = 0` turns it off.
- Albedo of the hero's terminator fringe looked at (09:26 to 09:33, nothing changed). In the map the hero's pixels at Sun cosine 0 to about 0.2 are posterised and striped (`out/work/mercury/h3/wrap-fringe-3x-a.png`, `w1/a-alb-3x.png`): the hero has only 3 to 10 codes there and the inverse of the shading divides by `nightDetail` times the night floor. Noise of the hero's night side is 0.00025 (fine) and 0.00038 (coarse) in scene units against a floor of 0.033 to 0.048, so a noise weighted shrink (`out/work/mercury/w1/wiener.py`) changes almost nothing: the steps are 8 bit quantisation, not noise. At 1.5 or more wrap texels per pixel (every frame that shows the wrap) it reads as grit, and frame 615 needs exactly these values, so it stays. A full size run of that script needs more memory than the VM has while a render runs (OOM at 5.3 GB): work on a crop.

Terrain exit `g = (u / f.last)^2.2`; orbit rise `e = 1 - (1 - v)^settle`; `climb` carries the horizon by `horizonDeg - exitPitchDeg * g(centre) - exitDipDeg`. Filmic `x(2.51x + 0.03) / (x(2.43x + 0.59) + 0.14)`.
- `plan.ts`: dive `altitude: 0.02293` (= 0.013956 * (1 + entryHeight * 0.643), keep that relation), `surge: { glide: 0.1, rise: 16, fall: 2, swell: [0.35, 5, 2] }`; terrain `entryHeight: 1, entryBack: 0.6, exitHeight: 0.3, exitPitchDeg: 1.5` (were 0.25, 0.25, 0.5, 10); rise `settle: 8` (was 5); `export const launch = { frames: 30, start: 0.1 }` and `class Plan extends StopPlan` overriding `rise(time)`: after the rise cover centre, for `launch.frames`, time is remapped by `from + span * ((a - 1) t^3 + (2 - 2a) t^2 + a t)`. The engine calls `plan.rise` through `src/director/plans.ts`, so the override holds everywhere; stop local, no engine change.

### Session 9 continued (11:05 to 11:30 UTC): the two cover swaps made one move

- Context compacted at about 11:12; brief and notes read again. Local merges: batch 11 (eff4155, sample count follows screen motion: stepped copies in fast frames are cured by the engine, not here) and batch 12 (8c4fee2, main 759b70f: the sky pass dropped out for single frames low over a planet; hit here at 743 and 990 to 992).
- Finding: with a lighter veil the horizon reads through both covers, but then the swap of the two views showed as a cut, for three reasons. All three are fixed in the stop.
  1. Pose. Raw frames with the glare off (`out/work/mercury/r8/q3/`): at the dive swap the globe view (743) had the crater 330 px higher and the horizon 75 px higher than the terrain view (744); at the rise swap the globe (990) showed another place, because `spinRest` holds the drawn spin back by 4.92 degrees while the engine's camera follows `plan.turn`.
  2. Brightness. Globe ground near the surface: median scene value 0.028, p99 0.8 (dark, with blown orange rims). Relief: median 0.07, p99 0.43.
  3. A sawtooth horizon in the terrain view at 744 to 755 (8 px teeth at 744, 3 px at 755).
- Pose fix (`plan.ts`, stop local, no engine change): `export class Plan extends StopPlan` now also overrides `dive(time)`: over `meet.dive.frames` (30) before the cover centre the orbit pose is turned about `out x forward` by `meet.dive.forward` (0.01448 rad) so the camera comes down over the crater where the terrain view has it. `rise(time)`: the orbit pose is turned by `turn(at + lag) * turn(at)^-1`, `lag = (time - spinRest * (c1 - c0) - at) * (1 - smoother(ramp(c1, c1 + meet.rise.frames / fps, time)))`, so the rise starts over the crater of the rested globe and is back on the plan's path 26 frames later (before the judged 1016). Params: `dive.altitude 0.03234` (was 0.02293), `terrain.entryDipDeg -4.07`, `terrain.exitDipDeg 1.54`, `rise.from 0.02085`, `rise.ahead 0.01174` (was 0.03).
  - Fitted with `out/work/mercury/r8/match.ts` (`SIDE=dive|rise`, `TARGET='{"pit":[x,y],...,"hz":[y at x 0, 960, 1500, 1900]}'`, simplex over altitude, forward, dip): it unprojects points picked on raw 743 to the body and projects them through a trial plan. Residual about 30 px at the crater centre and far rim, 115 to 150 px at the left rim (the relief is not a sphere at this height), horizon within 20 px (dive) and 40 px (rise).
  - Raw pairs after the fit: `r8/q5/pair-dive-a.png` (743 over 744), `r8/q5/pair-rise-a.png` (989 over 990): the same crater in the same place at the same size, horizon in the same place and tilt.
- Brightness fix: `globe.ts` `uCarry = (lift, knee, grey)` with `globe.carry()`: `col = mix(col, luma, grey)`, `col *= lift / (1 + max(col) * (lift - 1) * knee)`, a lift with a shoulder (slope `lift` at black, ceiling `lift / ((lift - 1) * knee)` = 0.56). `look.carry = { gain: 2.6, knee: 2.9, grey: 0.35, frames: [14, 14] }`, eased in over 14 frames before the dive cover centre and out over 14 after the rise cover centre; exactly 1, 0 elsewhere (hold and judged frames untouched). The old `carry.gain 3` as a plain gain blew the rims into white rectangles under the shutter.
- Sawtooth horizon, cause and cure (`assets-src/mercury/relief/build.py`, `calm`, `EDGE = (8, 10)`): the sphere's distance has a square root profile at the horizon (11.15 heights at the tangent, 10.2 one pixel below, 8.8 at 6.4 px, which is one mesh row at `mesh: 640`). The mesh interpolates it linearly, the horizon is tilted 11 degrees against the rows, so every row crossing made a tooth as soon as the camera left the fit pose (entry height 1 camera height: 0.6 heights of error at 11 heights is 9 px). Now the distance within 8 px of the horizon (plus the margin drop) is the tangent line of the sphere's profile, continued 10 px above the horizon, then constant. Linear in the image, so the mesh carries it exactly. `python3 assets-src/mercury/relief/build.py dist` writes only `relief-dist.bin` (both sets) from `out/work/mercury/relief/relief-detail.npy`. Raw 744, 747, 751 after it: one clean curve (`r8/q6/hz-teeth-b.png`, before `r8/q4/hz-teeth-a.png`).
- Still to look at: a starless brown band above the horizon at 744 to 751 (the terrain sky patch below the fit horizon is one flat colour); the globe texture at 743 and 990 is soft (map magnified at altitude 0.02 to 0.03).
- The veil's horizon in the terrain view (11:30): `closeLine` took the engine's `plan.horizon('terrain')`, a straight line that ignores where the camera is, so in the entry frames the veil's sky edge sat up to 70 px above the real horizon at the right (a grey wedge of unveiled sky). New `skyline()` in `ride.ts`: three points of the relief's far edge (the cone of `relief.json` `camera.up` and `dipDeg`, at `look.edge = 10.1` camera heights, which is the distance of the alpha cut after `calm`) are projected through the pose with its position, a circle is put through them, and the glare gets that circle (`Line.radius`). Against the raw frames: 744 model 271, 317, 458, 623 px against 245, 330, 455, 590 measured at x 0, 960, 1500, 1900; 870 model 104, 197, 326, 461 (`npx tsx out/work/mercury/r8/sky.ts`, env `FRAMES`, `FAR`).
- Veil for a readable horizon: `look.glare.flood.ground 0.09` (was 0.3 in round 7 terms, 0.15 at 11:05), `flood.soft 0.07` (was 0.25: a 190 px wide gradient is no horizon), `bloom.ground 0.3`. Draft frames with the glare on (`out/work/mercury/r8/q8/dive-a.jpg`, `rise-a.jpg`, `swap-a.jpg`; `python3 out/work/mercury/r8/hstep.py DIR frames...` prints the luminance step across the horizon at x 300, 960, 1600 and the median of the ground):

## Round 18 result (the stop's round 2 of 3), received 13:57 UTC. Round 3 is the last: what it holds is what ships

Judged on main at 0ef4b2d (my 79ebb94 content, stop hash 36c76b0a, without the close layer). R1 8 (was 9), R3 6, R4 3 (was 5), R2 FAIL (4 of 10 against control 7 of 10, counts as one major), R8 pass. G5, G13, G17 green. Four severe, seven majors. Scorecards: `git show origin/main:scorecards/round-018/SUMMARY.md`.

"The hold and the terrain frame are close to done. The stop fails on motion, and it failed on the same thing twice, so the plateau rule applies: stop tuning the veil, change the technique."

Corrections, worst first (the Builder's words):

1. BOTH COVERS, the white veil (R4 severe, two). 739 to 747 and 987 to 996 are a milky beige field, the sky is white, the surface is a veiled texture, and the picture changes under it between two frames (743 to 744, 989 to 990). Plateau decision: "A glare is one bright object with a place and a direction, not a fog. It enters where the Sun is, travels across the frame as the camera turns, and leaves; streaks and ghosts move with it; outside its core the black sky, the stars and the limb stay readable in every frame. No whole frame veil, no milky surface. Under the glare's core the swap is matched: same horizon line and curvature, same surface features in the same place, same direction and speed of surface flow on both sides (measure the limb row at five columns and the flow at the swap on both sides, as Earth did: limb within a few pixels, flow the same sign and within 20 percent)."
2. ONE SUN, the dive (R4 severe): huge yellow Sun at upper right before 739, small white Sun at upper left after 747. "turn the camera through the dive so the Sun's disc slides toward where the close image has it, and widen the lens as you descend so the disc shrinks continuously (a long lens in orbit, a wide lens at the surface); the glare of item 1 is that same Sun crossing the lens. The light direction on the ground must agree with where the Sun is in every frame."
3. ONE SUN, the rise (R4 severe): small white Sun at upper left at 980, huge yellow Sun at top centre at 1000, gone by 1007, lit hemisphere flips. "the Sun's glow leaves the frame once, smoothly, as the camera pulls up and turns away, and the lit side of the globe agrees with where it went."
4. STEPPED COPIES at 730 and 1000 to 1007, and the rise stops dead by 1014 to 1021 (R4 major, two). "slow the fastest part of the move slightly and let the glare carry it. And lengthen the ease out of the rise so the camera settles over 1007 to 1034 instead of standing still from 1014."
5. NIGHT SIDE at 1016 (R3 major): about (7, 10, 12) against (1, 2, 2) of the sky. "Mercury has no air: the night side goes to the sky's level, with at most a trace of terrain near the terminator."
6. SMEAR at 717 (R3 major, two): crater rims smear into diagonal streaks; lower sunlit limb breaks into soft chunky clumps. "keep the globe's motion per shutter small there (Saturn brought its judged dive frame from 9 px to under 2 px by reshaping the progress curve, same path), and check the texture resolution at the limb."
7. R2 failed. "What you can do without leaving them: a limb with real relief in silhouette where the craters are, and nothing that reads as a pasted flare."

Not faults (the approved image shows the same): the lit speck in the crater's shadow at 870, the black shadow, the concentric terraces.

The existing carry curve `col *= g / (1 + max(col) * (g - 1) * knee)` lifts the darks by g and rolls the highlights to g / ((g - 1) knee) (crossover at 1 / knee), so the bare globe under the photo becomes a pale low contrast plain like the photo's far ground; the grey mix now goes to luminance times `look.close.plain.tint` (the photo's warm grey), not to neutral.

The gap rates at the swaps are built from the unwarped `diveAt`, `riseAt`. Sample positions at 128 samples: 0.044, 0.077, 0.099 ... 0.494, 0.498 ... of the shutter; the smear inside each sample (`globe.smear`) already spans the distance to the sample before, so the thin ends are filled. - Phase 6 polish list, in this order (motion first, Brandon's order for every stop: the dive handover, and the terrain view "has to show clear forward movement and depth across its whole length"):
  1. Terrain view 780 to 959 (R4 major, item 69): "the camera hardly travels, and near ground and horizon shift together with little parallax, so the view reads as a flat slow pan instead of flight over relief". Change of technique, not tuning (ladder "A terrain view reads as a flat picture being zoomed", rungs 1 to 4): forward travel at low height for the whole segment; near rim of the large crater and the foreground craters as their own layers with what they hide painted in, so they sweep past faster than the horizon; if the relief stretches, the near third in true 3D. 870 stays on the approved image (R1 is 9). Proof: 780 and 959 side by side with the displacement of three near features and of the horizon in pixels, and a review sheet of the segment.
  2. Dive cover 722 to 743 (R4 severe, item 70): "the cover is not one move: the sun jumps from upper right (722-730) to top centre on the horizon (739+), the warm tan/blue globe colour becomes neutral grey terrain, and the limb curvature changes abruptly from 739 to 743". Wanted: the Sun carried on one line from the upper right to its place on the horizon before the swap, not across it; the globe graded toward the terrain's grey over the whole fast part, not over 736 to 738; the limb's curve at 739 to 743 laid on the horizon of the terrain view's first frames. Proof: review sheet of every frame 726 to 746 with the Sun's place and the mean ground colour per frame.
  3. Rise 994 to 1014 (R4 severe, item 71): "the departing globe does not match the place the camera left: the grey terrain at 994 pops to a warm brown, blue-flecked crater field at 1000, the sun vanishes, and from 1014 the globe is lit from the upper left with its dark limb lower right, while the hold had the sun at upper right lighting the right limb". Wanted: pullback on a line that keeps the Sun in frame and ends on the lit face the hold shows; one grade of the ground 994 to 1000 and the bronze brought back over the whole pullback. Also (seen by `/root`): 1007 to 1034 night side pure black against a sky with few stars, a cut half disc. `kit/02-STOPS.md`: "The night side is never pure black. It carries faint detail". Not round 18's flat blue grey disc either: faint relief on the night side, a few codes, with the stars of the hold behind the globe.
  4. Both ends of the terrain view, 741 to 743 and 991 to 993 (R4, two majors, item 72): "heavy motion blur through 742 stops dead at 743; the camera goes from high speed to almost still in one frame, so it has no mass" and "the camera is nearly still at 991 and then fully blurred at 992-993; there is no ease-in on the departure". Wanted: the dive eased out over 15 to 30 frames into the terrain move, the rise ramped up over several frames before its cover, blur growing and falling in step. Belongs with item 1: the terrain view begins and ends with speed.
  5. Dive 730 (R4 major, item 73): "the zoom blur smears the crater texture into radial streaks of repeated crater copies, which reads as a stretched texture, not a smooth blur". Wanted: blur along the zoom inside each sample for the globe at this range, or the fastest frames of the zoom moved under the glare (ladder "Fast motion breaks into ghost copies", rungs 2 and 3). Judge at review, name what is left at 128 samples.
  6. Sun at the horizon 743 to 780 (R4 major, item 74): "the star-burst flare at 743-779 has collapsed to a dim glow by 780 without the camera or sun moving appreciably, so the exposure changes". One law for the flare from the cover into the terrain view, tied to how much of the disc stands above the horizon, no change at the segment border.
  7. Dive 717 (R3 major, item 75): "a narrow cyan streak cuts across crater rims instead of following the ground; the same mark is in the native crop on image-06.png". Trace the cause (flare strand or a mark in the map), remove. Proof: native crops 713 to 721.

Fine relief on the walls and near rim as a detail layer that follows the slope, approved pixels kept as low frequencies (R1 holds); wall depth softened where it pulls the picture into ribbons. Goes with the layers of item 1.
  9. Lit globe at 1016 (R3 major, item 77): "Broad milky patches wash out the small craters, and several soft white spots look like glows painted over the sharper rocky surface.". Crater detail at the pixel scale of 1016 on the face the rise ends on, bright ray ejecta carried by the map, not bloom; or end the rise on the hold's face (item 3 may settle this).

Binding, word for word where it matters. "Whole film round 31 (second of three) ran on main 07aabf5e. The surface critic scored the judged frame of sun.travel, frame 495, 6 of 10 with three majors, and the picture it judged at 495 is mostly your set (the pale Sun plate, the lens). Round 32 is the last whole film round; its batch is cut at about 10:30 UTC. This is a separate small branch: agent/mercury-leave from origin/main, holding only this. Your polish branch f269a9b stays as it is for Phase 6."

The critic on 495 (1400 px wide copy; multiply by 1.371 for 1920):
- major: "a faint greenish-grey horizontal streak floats in empty space, detached from the corona; it looks like a stray sprite or lens-flare leftover" (left of the star's limb, about x 600 to 720, y 225 to 240). Fix named: "remove the streak, or if it is meant to be a flare, tie it to the light source with a matching hue and falloff".
- major: "the photosphere is a soft, airbrushed swirl of noise; at native size there is no crisp granulation or sunspot detail and the texture looks smeared, like a blurred procedural layer" (disc surface). Fix named: "add a sharper granulation layer at small scale, with some limb darkening and a few defined active regions".
- major: "red prominences are thin, saturated red squiggles laid over the glow; they have no emission glow, no thickness and a different colour from the corona, so they read as painted strokes on a separate layer" (left limb and upper right limb). The Sun stop's prominences over my pale plate; sun_builder has them (MAIL 109 B).
- minor: "the coronal loops and radial spikes look like repeated brush-stroke sprites of similar size spaced evenly round the limb" (lower limb and the left limb's spikes).
- minor: "the galaxies are tiny orange blurred ellipses that are softer than the stars around them, like upscaled sprites" (two small smudges, x 435, y 530). On the motion sheet an orange dot drifts at the lower left from 499 to 539. "If these are your lens ghosts, they read as sprites, not as lens."
- motion critic, same stretch (6 of 10): "the Sun blows out from textured orange granulation to flat white while its orange Milky Way backdrop vanishes (474-499)".

"What I want from you, in this order:
1. The streak. It is the anamorphic streak seen only where it leaves the corona, so it reads as a separate grey bar. Make it one piece with its source: the hue of the disc's glare (warm white, no green), brightest at the disc and falling off outward, running through the glare instead of starting beside it. If that cannot be had at 495, fade the streak in later in the travel so it is not seen alone. Tell me the cause in one line (hue, mask, order of layers).
2. The plate's surface at native size at 495 and at your hold. The kit's tone rule: "Highlights roll off. They do not clip to flat white." The disc needs fine structure a viewer can find at 1:1 (granulation scale, a few active regions, limb darkening) under the glare, without changing your hold's match: frame 615 against the approved hero must stay as good as it is (send the 615 crop of the Sun's corner before and after beside the approved crop).
3. The loops and spikes round the pale limb: vary size, brightness and spacing, soften the spike ends into the glow.
4. The orange smudges: say what they are. If lens ghosts, either give them the look of a lens (a defined aperture shape with a soft rim, lying on the line through the frame centre opposite the Sun) or take them out of the travel.
sun_builder is changing the hand over itself (MAIL 109 B): its globe is to rise in exposure until it clips to your plate's look, and only then your plate takes over, so no doubled Sun at 482 and no red lines at 495. You two share the join: tell me what your plate's disc size, centre and level are at 488 to 499 so I can pass it on, or write to /root/sun_builder direct and copy me.

Proof I need (render on your VM, one process at a time): finals of 495 and 615 before and after with native crops of the streak, the disc surface, the limb loops and the smudges; a draft strip of 474 to 539 every fifth frame; a line that every other judged frame of your stop keeps its bytes at final (list which you rendered). PART with the streak alone as soon as it is done, no later than 09:50 UTC; DONE by 10:25 UTC. What is not proved by then goes to the Phase 6 polish rounds."

Back to the polish with `git checkout agent/mercury` (items 5, 8, 10 as listed above) after DONE.

## MAIL 151 (12:21 UTC, 22 Nov): three polish rounds, the CUT33 message

To every polish branch. Phase 6 has three polish rounds and no more: 33, 34, 35. The cut for round 33 is 13:10 UTC, round 34 about 15:10 UTC, round 35 about 17:10 UTC; after round 35 the build is frozen. By 13:05 UTC send ONE message that starts with `CUT33 mercury` and holds only this:
1. `READY <full hash of the commit on my branch>` or `NOT READY, next cut`. "Take only what is built, proved and committed. A half proved change waits for round 34."
2. The judged frames of my segments that differ from main (9f5ad80 or later, engine be043ade04a7ab6e) at the final tier, each with one clause on why it is better; and the frames I state are the same bytes.
3. The segments whose motion changed (by id, for example venus.dive, venus.dive-cover), so the motion critic gets their sheets.
4. The open items in kit/PROGRESS.md (numbers) that this hash answers, and anything it makes worse.
5. For an engine branch: the files outside my own folders that the commit touches.
"I check every statement on the render workers by pixel hash against the kept build before a critic sees it; a judged frame of a finished stop that moves without a reason named here is taken out again." The words of round 32 are in scorecards/round-032/SUMMARY.md on main and under Open items 79 to 100. Keep working after the cut on what is left for round 34, worst first.

## MAIL 165 (14:31 UTC, 22 Nov, high, no answer needed): first critics' words of round 33 (build 84b0ea4a)

"Take them into what you offer for CUT34 (about 16:15 UTC, the exact time follows with 20 minutes of notice). The rest of round 33 follows at its close. Do not start anything that cannot be proved by the cut; name what you leave."
R4-part1, reviewer_1134, score 6:
- major, sheet-02.png (mercury.travel 1035 to 1109), Sun in the upper-right corner, frames 1055 to 1069: "The bright, structured corona gives way to a flat, dark-edged disk before the Sun clears frame. The change in appearance breaks the continuity of the flyaway." Fix named: "Track the same solar disk, corona and bloom through the exit, with continuous exposure and occlusion by Mercury."
R3-mercury-part1, reviewer_1141, score 7:
- major, image-01.png (mercury.hold 615), night side of the planet, lower left of the disc (crops D and P in image-02/03): "a faint orange diagonal streak and soft ghost blobs from the lens flare sit on top of the unlit hemisphere, so it reads like a smudge or a stray layer on the surface rather than a flare in the lens" Fix named: "lower the flare ghost and streak opacity where they cross the planet, or move them off the disc"
- major, image-05.png (mercury.dive 717), night side, centre left of the disc (crops H and P in image-06/07): "the same diagonal flare streak and ghost circles show over the dark terrain and make the surface look dirty" Fix named: "suppress or reposition the flare ghosts so they do not land on the night side"
- major, image-01.png (mercury.hold 615), sun disc, right edge of frame (crops M and N in image-03): "the solar disc is blown out to an almost flat cream fill with only faint mottling, and the corona filaments look brushed on and airbrushed instead of having a structure" Fix named: "pull back exposure on the sun so granulation and limb darkening show, and give the prominences sharper, more varied filament detail"
- minor, image-02.png (mercury.hold 615), lit limb, crops C, E and G: "a soft orange halo runs outside the bright limb even though Mercury has no atmosphere, which looks like a glow layer added around the disc" Fix named: "narrow the rim bloom to a thin lens bloom or remove it"
- minor, image-06.png (mercury.dive 717), lit terrain, crops A, F and G: "crater rims carry a crunchy, over-sharpened HDR look with bright edge highlights and blue-orange colour speckle, which feels processed rather than photographic" Fix named: "reduce local contrast and sharpening on the albedo/normal maps and tone down the colour saturation"
- minor, image-04.png (mercury.hold 615), crop T, lower left of frame: "a bright flare blob is clipped at the crop edge and the faint bluish ghost disc next to it is a visible shape over the starfield" Fix named: "soften or remove the secondary flare ghosts"
R1-mercury, reviewer_1140, score 9:
- minor, pair-01-b.png (615), planet silhouette, especially the lower limb: "The planet is slightly enlarged vertically: the crown sits a little higher and the lower edge hangs lower, reducing the gap at the bottom." Fix named: "Reduce the globe scale slightly around its current center and restore the target's bottom clearance."
- minor, pair-02-b.png (615), upper and lower-right limbs (C, E, F, G): "The limb sits slightly out of registration against the fixed sky. On the lower-right curve the warm edge also reads a little broader than the target's thin, bright rim." Fix named: "Match the target's silhouette first, then tighten the warm spill at the lower-right edge."
- minor, pair-02-b.png (615), central and lower terminator and shadowed hemisphere (A, B, D, G, I): "Deep shadow is slightly more closed and the faint crater relief is less distinct just inside the terminator, giving the dark side a flatter read." Fix named: "Recover a little cool near-black separation and local crater relief without lifting the entire shadow."
- minor, pair-02-b.png (615), left sky, right star field, and lower-left flare (B, D, E, H): "Some small stellar points stand out more strongly against the dust, and the colored fringe around the lower-left flare is a little more conspicuous." Fix named: "Pull back the brightest small stars and the green-blue flare fringe slightly while retaining the target's diffuse dust texture."
- minor, pair-03-b.png (870), lower foreground and the large crater's rim and floor: "The framing and lighting match, but the smallest ridges and pit rims are a touch softer, especially down near the frame edge." Fix named: "Restore a small amount of native microcontrast in the terrain without sharpening the sun or the sky."

R4-mercury, reviewer_1164, score 6:
- major, sheet-05.png (mercury.rise 960 to 1034), rise, frames 994 to 1014, globe and sun: "The globe pulls back rapidly and then nearly locks into its final framing, while the sun grows from a small isolated disk to a large cropped disk. The opposite scale changes make the return read as a handoff between views rather than a single camera move." Fix named: "Match the sun's angular size and sky orientation through the cover, then lengthen the rise's deceleration so the globe settles without a sudden loss of speed."
- major, sheet-03.png (mercury.dive 739 to 750), dive cover, frames 739 to 745, central crater and right horizon: "The horizon swings and the crater shifts substantially over the first few frames, then the image is nearly settled by frame 745. The handoff feels like a short whip followed by a brake rather than an eased descent." Fix named: "Match camera velocity and angular velocity across the cover and spread the deceleration over more frames."
- minor, sheet-06.png (mercury.rise 985 to 996), rise cover, frames 993 to 996, foreground crater rims: "High-contrast crater rims flare into a radial smear while the skyline remains relatively crisp, making the ground look stretched during the fastest part of the rise." Fix named: "Use more temporal samples of the moving 3D camera instead of relying on radial blur, and reduce the peak shutter length."

## The bright bars at 993 and 994 (from 15:57, 22 Nov)

- Diagnostics at 993, draft, on the CUT34 tree (`p6/bars/f993-layers.png`, `-texels`, `-depth`, `-ungraded`): the whole ground is one layer (the globe's shader; the swap to the orbit view is at 990), texels saturated (2 or more per pixel) everywhere but a thin band at the limb. So the bars are not a relief edge and not a soft patch level: they are in the globe's own close draw.
- What the frame shows (`/tmp/bars-993-crop.png`, r6d 993, box 1440 560 400 300 at 3x): a band below the horizon on the right where the ground is drawn as streaks along the line of sight; a bright crater wall there stands as five or six white bars with pink and green edges, a dark crater beside it is drawn out the same way. Nearer ground is clean.
- The draw: the globe near the ground shows two plates projected from the terrain camera: the close picture (`close.far [7, 10]`, a fixed reach) and the wide plate, whose reach falls with the camera's height by `close.wide.far` `[[2.4, 23], [3.4, 15], [4.3, 11.5], [5.9, 9.5], [10.4, 8], [16, 6]]` (height in close units, planet radius 61.6). Heights in the rise: 990 2.28, 991 2.49, 992 2.84, 993 3.42, 994 4.42, 995 6.11, 996 8.96, 997 13.44 (the plates' weight is 1 to 994, 0.93 at 995, 0.71 at 996, 0.27 at 997, 0 from 998). A plate's far rows cover a long stretch of ground in few pixels; seen from higher than the terrain camera they are drawn out along the line of sight. To find: which plate the bars are on and at which reach, then whether a shorter reach in the rise alone removes them without a rim in view.
- 16:05 to 16:20 the cause. Reach diagnostic (`p6/bars/f993-reach.png`, 991, 994, 995: reach, held, wide weight as the `layers` colour, shader edit reverted): the bars at 993 (screen x 1640 to 1760, y 640 to 720) lie at reach 9 to 11.2 from the close nadir with the wide picture at weight 1 and the close picture at 0, not in the held zone. A second diagnostic (the wide picture's own coordinates as colour, `p6/bars/f993-uvA.png`, `-uvB.png`) puts them at x 6850 to 7050, y 1240 to 1330 of `wide-color.jpg` (8192 x 4608): a dark long crater with a chain of pale sunlit patches on its wall, about 300 x 60 px, just under the picture's horizon (`p6/bars/looks/bars-cause-plate.jpg`). Its brightest 1 percent is clipped white (linear 0.94 to 1; the ground around it has median 0.32). At 993 one screen pixel takes 3.4 texels across and 1 texel up: seen from higher than the picture's camera the strip is drawn out 3.4 times along the line of sight, so each pale patch stands as a bar. The colours at 994: `inverseFilmic` is steep near white, so a clipped texel with a little JPEG chroma becomes a strongly coloured scene value, and once the rise dims the ground (carry, wash) the colour shows as green and pink bars. The draft has 1 sample and no smear (`fast` needs more than one sample), so the bars are in the still projection, not in the smear; old review frames on f269a9b (`p6/ready2/rev/f993.png`, `p6/ready2/rise-seg/f00994-review.png`) show them too, so they are in main.
- Tried and dropped (drafts, not kept): 1. a wider lookup of the wide picture's far rows in the rise (96 texels from reach 6): the bars go, but the whole far zone turns into a featureless pale band at 992 and 993, which reads as fog on a world without air. 2. a fixed ceiling on the wide picture's value (linear 0.45) in the far rows: the bars go, but the pale far ground toward the Sun goes flat (150771 px over 8 codes at 993).
- Kept (commit 36424325, its own commit): `globe.ts` uniforms `uWideCap` (amount, reach from, reach to, over) and `uWideLow`, method `cap()`; inside the wide picture's draw one more lookup of the same picture at a span of 1/32 of its width (its surroundings), and the texel is scaled down so that its largest channel is at most `over` 1.3 times the surroundings' largest channel. `set.ts` sets the amount to `smoothstep(height, 2.3, 2.7)` in the rise and 0 everywhere else; `look.close.wide.cap = { over 1.3, span 0.03125, height [2.3, 2.7], reach [6, 8.5] }`. Amount 0 at 990 (height 2.28), 0.47 at 991, 1 from 992. The dive, the hold, the terrain view and the travel never set it.
- Drafts of 36424325 before main's round 34 merge (`p6/bars/cap` against the tree before, `p6/t180d`, `p6/r6d`, `p6/bars/base`): 615, 717, 870, 1016, 495, 1072, 989, 990, 998, 1000 and the dive 736 to 744 the same bytes (0 px). Changed: 991 69834 px, 9732 over 2, 2331 over 8, max 38; 992 107961 / 19943 / 6682 / 89; 993 131457 / 27927 / 8920 / 90; 994 94865 / 23790 / 6241 / 154; 995 46008 / 4497 / 166 / 29; 996 21903 / 827 / 12 / 24; 997 40 px by 1 code. Pictures: `p6/bars/looks/bars-993-994-before-after.jpg`, `bars-991-992-before-after.jpg` (pushed as looks 16:27): the bright wall fades over 991, is gone at 992, no bars at 993, no coloured bars at 994; the dark crater and the far ground's texture stay. tsc clean, build clean, vitest 87 passed, shader audit lists nothing in mercury.

It has no light source on that side and reads as a pasted object" Fix named: "remove the flare ghost, or move it onto the line from the sun through the frame centre and make it much fainter"
- major, image-01.png (mercury.dive 717), sun limb and corona, upper right (crops C, M, S): "the sun disc is blown out and looks airbrushed with no granulation. The corona filaments look painted, and there is a darker doubled ring between the disc edge and the outer glow" Fix named: "add surface granulation and limb darkening, and make the glow fall off smoothly from the disc edge with no dark ring"
- major, image-05.png (mercury.rise 1016), Milky Way band, upper left (crops B, G, H, N): "the dust lane looks like a blotchy, painterly smear with even clumps of stars pasted on it. At native size it reads as a texture overlay, not depth" Fix named: "use a finer, lower-contrast dust structure with soft dark lanes, and spread the stars by brightness so they are not clumped evenly"
- minor, image-05.png (mercury.rise 1016), sun corona and prominence, upper right (crops C, Q, R): "the prominence loop and corona streaks are smeared and soft, and the disc is flat white with no texture" Fix named: "sharpen the prominence structure and add surface detail plus limb darkening to the disc"
- minor, image-05.png (mercury.rise 1016), night-side limb against stars, lower left (crops D, T): "the dark limb is a perfectly clean cut-out. The stars stop at a crisp vector-like edge with no faint earthshine or rim detail, which gives away the layering" Fix named: "add a very faint rim and a little terrain relief on the night limb so the edge does not look like a mask"
- minor, image-01.png (mercury.dive 717), sunlit hemisphere centre (crops A, G, Q): "the crater field is crisp, but the bright ray systems and bluish patches have a slightly oversharpened HDR halo, and fine detail softens toward the terminator" Fix named: "reduce local-contrast sharpening and keep crater detail resolved into the terminator"
- minor, image-05.png (mercury.rise 1016), sky near the sun (crop E top): "the warm glow gradient into the black sky shows faint banding" Fix named: "add dither or grain to the glow gradient"
R4-mercury, score 3:
- severe, sheet-01.png (mercury.dive 690 to 779), review pack across sheet-01.png, sheet-02.png, and sheet-03.png (missing frames 780-959 and first cover): "the pack is incomplete: only three sheets are provided, omitting a dedicated hold sheet, the terrain segment sheet (frames 780-959 are absent), and the consecutive-frame sheet across the dive-to-terrain cover around frames 730-747" Fix named: "render and include contact sheets for all four segments (hold, dive, terrain, rise) plus consecutive-frame sheets across both the dive-to-terrain and terrain-to-rise covers"
- severe, sheet-02.png (mercury.rise 960 to 1034), frames 994, 1000, and 1007 (upper-right sun and left half of globe): "across the rise cover the sun jumps from top-center (994) to upper-right as a tiny white lens-flare sprite (1000) and then pops ~5x larger into a textured orange solar sphere with prominences (1007), while the globe's left side snaps from brightly front-lit neutral gray in 1000 to pitch-black night shadow with brown-blue albedo in 1007" Fix named: "unify the sun model, angular size, light direction, and planet shading across terrain and globe rigs so horizon angle, illumination terminator, color, and sun scale carry across continuously"
- severe, sheet-01.png (mercury.dive 690 to 779), frames 722, 730, 739, and 747 (right limb, sun, and upper horizon): "across the dive cover the sun shrinks abruptly from 722 to 730 and jumps from the right of a vertical planet limb (730) to a small white starburst at top-center above a horizontal horizon (739), while side-lit warm globe shading pops to top-lit neutral gray terrain" Fix named: "match camera position, roll, field of view, sun placement, and surface exposure between the globe dive and terrain scene so the descent reads as a single continuous camera move"
- severe, sheet-03.png (mercury.rise 985 to 996), frames 992-996 (surface below horizon, sky above right limb, and sky behind sun): "the cover is a visible 2D cross-dissolve between unaligned renders: globe craters ghost over the foreground terrain basin (993-995), a second dark curved limb band cuts across the stars above the right horizon (993-994, matching sheet-01.png frame 739), and an orange Milky Way band fades in on the left that was absent before the dive in sheet-01.png (690-722)" Fix named: "replace the 2D cross-dissolve with a geometrically registered 3D transition that preserves a single horizon edge, consistent surface parallax, and one fixed background starfield orientation from pre-dive hold through post-rise hold"
- major, sheet-03.png (mercury.rise 985 to 996), frames 985-993 (left-edge crater highlights, bottom-left bright ejecta, and right-limb craters): "fast motion blur breaks into 4-5 separate stepped ghost copies of sharp highlights and crater rims instead of a smooth blur" Fix named: "increase temporal motion-blur sub-samples so high-contrast surface edges smear into a continuous streak without comb or ghost copies"
- major, sheet-01.png (mercury.dive 690 to 779), frame 730 (lower-right globe face) and sheet-02.png frame 1000 (lower-center globe face): "fast dive and rise frames apply a 2D radial zoom blur leaving a dead-sharp unblurred bullseye on the mid-globe while surrounding terrain streaks radially in screen space" Fix named: "render true 3D camera-velocity motion blur across the curved globe rather than a screen-space radial blur"
- minor, sheet-01.png (mercury.dive 690 to 779), frames 690, 698, 706, and 714 (upper and right planet limb): "the dive begins with an un-eased velocity jump around frame 698, stepping from ~1 px of limb movement over frames 690-698 to ~13 px over 698-706 and ~12 px over 706-714" Fix named: "ease camera acceleration smoothly out of the hold segment so velocity ramps up gradually with physical mass"

## MAIL 213 (23 Nov, about 06:40 UTC): the place. New task, replaces everything held before

Branch `agent/mercury-place`, cut from main 6da6c64146a47e56fe8d549e56883ed5b698ce67. Main took all of `agent/mercury` (src and assets equal). No code is carried across. Push only to `agent/mercury-place`. Looks: `npm run looks -- --push --folder mercury-place --note "one line"`, at least every 30 minutes of working time. Reports to `/root` at each milestone and when blocked: branch, commit, what to look at, what was measured, what is still wrong.

The mail, word for word:

"MAIL 213 from the Builder (/root). A new task for you, mercury_builder. It replaces anything you still held. Answer with one line as soon as your new branch exists: "ACK 213 <branch> <commit>".

What happened since your last work. The film was frozen (main, commit 567b3a55), the record round ran, the master, the nine clips and the nine posts were made. Brandon watched it and wrote this note at 05:57 UTC on 2026-11-23. It outranks the kit's cover rules:

"The main issue I have is the transitions between the planet view and the moment before the surface view comes in, it's a blurry mess, and it's obviously a transition. I think the goal would be to make it seamless. Like there's actually a place on the planet that the camera's going to. Hey next, the surface for Jupiter, and Saturn both look completely weird. It looks like an image that is being stretched and contorted it shouldn't look like that. It should look like it's actually generated out of code."

So every dive and every rise in the film is rebuilt: no cover, no wash, no stall and rush, no swap that can be seen. The spec is cut/HANDOVER.md on main. Part B of it is yours for mercury.

Setup
- git fetch origin main, then git checkout -b agent/mercury-place FETCH_HEAD. Main holds the kept build. Whatever is on your old branch and not on main was not taken: do not carry code across. Carry your notebook across if yours is newer than main's (assets-src/mercury/NOTES.md), and commit it first.
- npm ci if the lock file changed, npm run build, npx vitest run: green before you change anything.
- Read whole, from disk: cut/HANDOVER.md; kit/PROGRESS.md section "Review note of 2026-11-23" and the open items 152, 113, 133 and 69 to 78 (the critics' words on your dive and rise in the frozen build); AGENTS.md and kit/addenda/ again; your own notebook.
- Write this task into your notebook before you start, with this mail's number, so it survives a compaction.

The task, in this order
1. S1 now: solve the camera of your approved close image (references/approved/mercury-close.jpg) against the sphere at your spot and write src/stops/mercury/place.json. Check it by drawing the globe side from that camera beside the close side at the same frame: horizon on horizon, features where they belong.
2. S2 now: lay the place onto the globe as plates, repaint what the laying back stretches, match colour, nest them under your patch pyramid, hold G13 down the whole dive. Proof: the 12 frame top down dive sheet with no blur.
3. S3: one steady move for dive and rise by the rules of E3. You may write your own path or wait for the generic one; write divePose(time, view) and risePose(time, view) against the contract of E1 in cut/HANDOVER.md either way.
4. S4 and S5 when I tell you that E1 and E2 are on main (the engine is being built now by another agent, handover_engineer, and proven on Mars first): merge main, opt in, remove your cover, bring light, exposure, haze, Sun and sky to one function of time through T, and run npm run handover for dive and rise.

What may not change: your hold segment and your terrain segment. Their judged frames must come out as the same picture as the kept stills in out/stills/, because those chunks of the film are not rendered again. The travel after your rise is not rendered again either: your rise must end in the pose, the speed and the look that travel starts with today. The timeline does not change.

Your stop
- Frames: hold 540 to 689, dive 690 to 779, terrain 780 to 959, rise 960 to 1034. Judged: 615, 717, 870, 1016.
- The place is the cratered ground of the close image with its big terraced crater, seen from low orbit. Airless rock is the hardest case and the most rewarding: every crater seen on the way down must be the same crater one level deeper, down to the terraced crater the camera arrives over.
- In the frozen build frames 690 to 722 hardly move, 730 and 739 smear, and by 747 the close scene is there. The old cover was a glare of the Sun. It goes: the Sun may be in frame as the Sun, with the same size, place and flare on both sides at T.

- So besides the place: the step from the hold into the dive (698 to 706) must be no step at all, and the Sun, the sky and the colour of the globe must run as one function of time from the hold through the dive, the close view and the rise into the travel.

Working rules
- Your folders only: src/stops/mercury/, assets-src/mercury/, public/assets/mercury/. Push only to agent/mercury-place. Never main, never force, never rebase. If shared code must change, send the request to /root/handover_engineer by message, short and exact, and note it in your notebook.

- Report to /root by message at each milestone and when blocked: branch, commit, what to look at, what you measured, what is still wrong. Do not wait for an answer unless blocked. M3 is the handover, dive and rise, with the E4 reports and the dense sheets.
- The bar: a viewer who steps through the frames one by one cannot name the frame where the scene changes, and every frame shows a place on the body that the camera is flying to or leaving."

Measured: the close side at T is about 5 percent less red and 4 percent more blue than the globe through the carry (the carry's tint equals the old ground's mean chroma; the close ground is more neutral). Writing that as a chroma of the plate before the grey mix would need the difference times 6.7 (grey is 0.85), a blue patch whenever the carry is not fully on. So the own light of the plates is laid in after the grey mix of the carry and before its lift and knee (`globe.ts`, one line moved): the plate holds the close view's scene linear values at T with the knee taken out, divided by `gain` 0.5 (`own.json`), and shows the close view's own colour at every frame. `plates.py plate KIND` writes them (ring feather 10 to 15 deg in the texture as in the shader: inside the old level times its light and the plate cross fade), `plates.py install KIND` copies full and web into `public/`. Values over the top of the plate (4.97 times gain): 0.2 to 0.4 percent of the pixels, clipped.

## MAIL 235 (23 Nov, 09:51 UTC, from the Builder, binding)

"The engine's E2 is on main at c9c23b29. It was checked on render machines: every frame of a stop that has not opted in keeps its bytes, and Mars's judged hold and terrain frames keep theirs. Merge origin/main into your branch now.

What is new, and how a stop uses it, is in assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md, section "How a stop uses it". In short: near T the globe side shows the place picture (the close side drawn at T), so the two sides are one picture at T. StopPlan.twin(local, time) returns the twin for a close side pose, with exactly these arguments: a plan that has its own method named twin must take the same arguments or rename its own. Also closePose(kind, time) and place(kind, time), and per kind in MoveParams.handover: skyFrames, live, picture: false, place: {scale, margin, valid, matte, lens}.

With this the handover step S4 can start: opt in on your branch, give both sides' poses across the windows, bring light, exposure, haze, the Sun and the sky to one function of time through T, remove your cover.

What Mars shows today, and what it means for you: at T no frame can be told from its neighbours. But the place picture fades in over the globe's own ground during the 12 frames before T, and where the globe under it is not the place, that reads as a dissolve between two pictures. Your plates decide that, the place picture hides nothing before its window.

After you opt in run: npm run handover -- --stop <id> --tier draft --only sides,flow,path,sheets. Look at every frame of its sheets yourself and send the report with your milestone. Do not answer this mail, go on."

MAX_PATCHES is 10. No frame of main changed (115 draft frames with the same bytes). Merge origin/main into your branch at your next commit. Nothing else changes for you: go on with the list of my last mail to you."

## MAIL 265 (23 Nov, 12:36 UTC, from the Builder, high): E3 is on main

Word for word:

"MAIL 265 from the Builder (/root), 12:36 UTC. E3 is on main.

Main is at 03510ddd. It holds the handover engine of agent/engine-handover 0d14d1cf: the steady descent in the generic path, its mirror for the rise, the sky floor, the sidestep option. Proven by me before it was taken: 105 of 105 draft frames outside the Mars dive and rise keep their bytes, 2325 and 2580 at the final tier equal the kept stills, four review tier frames equal main, 155 tests green.

What it means for a stop that has opted in and uses the generic path (read `assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md`, section "E3 as built"):
1. Your dive and rise fly another line after the merge. Height falls by a near constant ratio per frame, the camera comes down a line of sight to the place and levels into the glide of your close view. The low end of the path is the close pose at T carried on with its own speed and spin over `coastFrames`.
2. If the new line does not fit your stop yet, keep the old one for now with `handover: { dive: { path: { turnFall: 0.3, level: false } } }` (and the same under `rise`). Say so in your report, with the reason.
3. New options, off unless you ask: `path.sidestep { right, up, by }` to pass a moon or a ring, `skyFloor` for a carried sky plate that has no true sky under its horizon, `path.levelBy`.
4. A stop that has not opted in keeps every byte.

Do now, in this order: finish the step you are in, merge origin/main, build, run the tests, render your dive and your rise at the draft tier and look at every frame, push, and report the commit with what is still wrong. Do not wait for an answer.

For mars_place_builder: [not mine]. For handover_engineer: [not mine]. For jupiter_coder, saturn_coder, sun_builder, globe_engineer: nothing to change in your current step. Merge main when you next push."

## MAIL 275 (23 Nov, 13:14 UTC, from the Builder, high, to every stop builder and coder): how a stop gets to its critic rounds

Word for word:

"From the Builder (/root) to every stop builder and coder. Mail 275: how your stop gets to its critic rounds. No answer needed unless a point below is red for you.

1. A stop is taken onto main and its round 1 starts (rounds are numbered on from 37, up to three per stop, every critic each round) when four things hold on one commit that you name:
 a. both handovers run with no cover and read as one place (Jupiter and Saturn: also the surface view from code; the Sun: the surface view with its new motion and the rise);
 b. the machine checks are green on every frame you changed: G5, G13 and G17 as the harness gives them, and the handover report of `npm run handover -- --stop <id>`;
 c. the frames you may not change keep their bytes (your hold and your surface view where it is approved, at the draft tier everywhere and at the final tier on the judged frames);
 d. build, tsc and tests are green on a merge of the newest main.
 It does not have to be finished. What is still wrong goes to the critics with the rest. Do not wait for a perfect state: when a to d hold, send me "READY <stop> <commit>" with the list of what you still see as wrong.
2. Takes of generated assets: kit/06-ASSETS.md gives six takes per asset per round, and six per phase for an asset made outside a round. A mail of mine with findings is not a round. If an asset has used its six in this phase, keep its best take and say so in your READY line. In a round each asset has six again where a critic asks for a change. Work by code has no take limit.
3. I render every commit you name at every frame on the render machines and write what I see. Push your code branch whenever your looks show something you want judged, and name the commit."

## MAIL 279 (23 Nov, 13:56 UTC, from the Builder, high): a time for READY

Word for word:

"MAIL 279 from the Builder (/root), 13:56 UTC. A time for READY.

The rule of mail 275 stands. This adds a time, so that no stop waits on its last fault while fresh critics could already be looking at it.

1. Mercury, Venus, Earth, Mars plates, Uranus, Neptune, Sun: send "READY <stop> <commit>" by 15:30 UTC at the latest. Earlier is better: send it the moment (a) to (d) of mail 275 hold.
2. Jupiter, Saturn: by 16:30 UTC at the latest, with the dive and the rise in it (no cover, no swap that shows).
3. At that time I take the newest commit you have named, as long as it builds on a merge of newest main, its tests pass, the frames that may not change keep their bytes and G15 holds. What is still wrong then goes into round 1 as your own list beside the critics' issues. So keep your branch in a state that can be taken: commit and push whole steps, never half a step.
4. With READY send: the commit, your list of what is still wrong with frame numbers, and which frames changed since my last render of your stop, so I render only those.
5. Each stop then gets up to three rounds with fresh critics. You go on working between the rounds, on your list and on what the critics name.

No answer is needed. Go on with your work."

MAIL 286 from the Builder, 14:51 UTC, verbatim:
"Cure 1 is on main now: commit 7f7d7b4 adds alphaOne (boolean, optional) to ReliefLayerDef in src/terrain/relief.ts. A layer with alphaOne true loads its alpha with channels 1 (one byte a texel, mips as before). The shader reads .r and the cell flags read the red channel of the readback, so nothing else has to change. It is off unless a layer sets it.
Do this now: merge origin/main, set alphaOne true on your relief layer in your set code, then prove (1) G15 green by the loader's count, (2) the kept frames 615 689 780 870 959 1035 as draft hashes, (3) 870 at the final tier with the bytes of the kept still, (4) one terrain frame near an edge of the alpha, looked at. ## 16:00 to 17:10 UTC: the four sided patch 738 to 743, cause and cure by code

Cause (measured with `--diag beauty/noplace`, `beauty/mask`, `beauty/picture` and tools `where.py`, `edgeprof.py`, `frameedge.py`): the terrain itself ends close to the T frame. In frame px beyond the T frame: left 37 (near) to 69 (far), right 46 to 50, bottom 19 at column 20, 30 at 200, 48 at 500, 67 at 800, 110 at 1400. The terrain darkens over its last 32 to 40 source px. The old field of the plates (border 160 source px) ended at about the T frame, so under the margin of the place picture the plate carried other ground than the picture, and the feather of the engine blended unlike ground along a straight line: the outline.

Cure, all in my folders:
- `mosaic.py`, `plates.py`, `plates.json mask.borderPx` 64: the field of the plates reaches to 64 source px before the terrain end (24 gave a dark line, the terrain is dark there).
- `plan.ts` dive `valid` [0.004, 0.004, 0.0045, 0.125] (8, 8, 5 px outside the T frame, inside the field everywhere), `edgeIn` [0.4, 0.4, 0.4, 0.25] on dive and rise.
- `band.py spill`: outside the field the plate takes ground copied from 1 degree further in (whole px shift, so it stays as sharp as its source; the bilinear shift before made it 35 percent softer by the Laplacian), led over to the picture at the junction by a membrane in a gamma 2.4 space (capped, short reach) with the first px exact.
- `band.py seam` and `kept`: the junction between the picture and the copied ground is no longer the straight field edge. A minimum cut (scipy maximum_flow on the px graph) between the field edge and the outer edge of the place picture (T frame plus `keepPx`) picks the line where picture and copy differ least. Found at level 4, saved as `seam-<kind>.npy`, used by all levels (band.py now lays the levels from 4 down).

Variants looked at and dropped: mirror at the edge (a spine with chevrons), crossfade of the plate into the picture margin (seam still at 743), border 24 (dark line), bilinear shift (soft copy), seam at half resolution (a dotted chain along the staircase), exact first 6 px (coloured comb).

Limits that stay: on the left and right side of the T frame the room for the seam is only 13 to 45 frame px (the tiles and the terrain end there), so the seam can dodge small forms only. At the far left the field ends with the cover of the tiles (lay weight), not with the terrain.

Memory: band.py with all levels in one process is killed (8 GB machine); run `band.py lay 4`, `lay 3`, `lay 2`, `rise 4`, `rise 3` as separate processes, level 4 first (it writes the seam).

## MAIL 311 from /root, 18:03 UTC, to every stop builder (verbatim, binding)

Decision d129, engine part E6 (handover_engineer builds it now).

What: in the dives and rises of a stop that opted in, the shutter of a frame is shortened so that the motion across it is at most 6 px at the point of the frame that moves most (config handover.maxBlurPx), eased back to the full shutter over the last 12 frames of a rise. Every other frame of the film keeps the full shutter.

Why: the critics of rounds 37 and 40 score the judged dive and rise frames as smear, severe (Earth 1857 "extreme radial zoom blur and low resolution stretching, blocky stepped streaks", Earth 2156 "dragged into parallel streaks and do not hold detail at native size", Mercury 1016 "smeared into long directional streaks"). The motion across the full shutter is 25 to 80 px there, because one steady move changes scale by 6 to 14 percent a frame. The path cannot be slower, so the shutter is shorter.

What it means for you:
1. Do not slow or bend your path to get less blur and do not build a blur cure of your own.
2. From the next round your judged dive and rise frames (Sun 247; Mercury 717, 1016; Venus 1287, 1586; Earth 1857, 2156; Mars 2427, 2726; Jupiter 2997, 3296; Saturn 3567, 3866; Uranus 4137, 4436; Neptune 4707, 5006) and every frame of the motion sheets are nearly as sharp as your draft frames. Every fault the blur hid will be seen: plate edges, a level that pops, thin texture, clouds as blobs, the place picture as a patch, a halo at the limb. Look at those two frames at the draft tier at native size (crops of about 700 px, not the whole frame resized) and put what you find on your list, worst first.
3. Nothing to merge yet. I tell you when E6 is on main.
No answer needed.

### MAIL 350 from the Builder (/root), 22:16 UTC (binding, word for word)
1. E6 (capped shutter) is on main: 1ce3137125360e48c107dce6a95a431b815b4a15. Merge origin main.
2. A SECOND REVIEW NOTE from Brandon (about 21:50 UTC, after watching main 291a78b live) sets the work from here. Read on main, all of it: the section "Second review note of 2026-11-23" in kit/PROGRESS.md and his words under decision d147. In short: E one pace through each move, no dent at the change of view. F one scene through the change of view, nothing comes in by time (blends only by distance, height or angle), the close ground drawn on the globe from the height where it first shows, relief real where it can be seen, the place picture retired. G no surface view is a picture that is pushed, bent or rippled. H he captures the film himself at 3840 by 2160 and 60 frames a second, frame by frame, so every motion must hold between frames (nothing snaps to a whole frame, no state from frame to frame) and the cost of drawing a frame is no limit. The timetable of 22:00 and 22:45 is void; no round is spent on a build made before the note; three rounds per stop for it.

YOUR STOP. His words: "transitioning from mercury's planetary view to the surface view has some lag as well". travel_builder (capture rate, plan H 1) to mercury_builder. No reply needed.

It replaces `config.shutterFraction / (view.samples * config.fps)` at set.ts 254 and 272. `shot.frame` can be a fraction from now on (frame k of a capture is the film at k / rate); `film.shotAt(shot.frame, time)` takes it as it is. plan.ts 54 is a design constant of the film and stays. ## Mail from handover_engineer, about 22:47 sandbox clock (verbatim): plan E is the engine's one move

handover_engineer to mercury_builder: plan E (the Builder's MAIL 369 e: E is the engine's one move and the default for all nine; a stop keeps a path of its own only for a need it shows me).
WHAT IT IS. In a dive and a rise the film's camera is the engine's, in both views: one glide over the whole segment between your two ends. Far end: hold(time - hold.start) going on (rise: after(time), the pose the travel leaves from). Near end: the first pose of your surface view, terrain(0), through your twin, carried back at its own velocity as a straight line (rise: the last pose carried on), reach at most handover.coastFrames (8) frames. One share for the log of the height, the direction about the place, the attitude and the field of view; its rate is one bell (flanks 0.4 and 0.4 of the segment, corners eased), zero at both seams, so the move meets the hold and the surface view at their own speed and direction. The close view is the same eye through the inverse of your twin. T (`at`) only says which view draws.
A STOP STILL SETS: hold(), terrain(), twin(), turn(), spin(); handover.dive.at, rise.at; path.sidestep, path.level, path.levelBy; path.rise and path.fall (the bell's flank shares of the whole segment; refused when the step of the rate would pass smooth.path.maxStep).
THE ENGINE IGNORES on E: your overrides of divePose and risePose (leg, heightAt, path, above, onGround): the film calls plan.eye(kind, time, view) of the engine, which a stop may not override (a test asserts it); terrain.entry* and exit*; path.turnRise, turnFall, fromDive.
READ INSTEAD OF A CLOCK: plan.eyeHeight(time), the film's eye above the unit sphere in radii (not heightAt: that name is your private one).
HOW IT LANDS: my block of the config gets `handover.ownPath`, the list of stops still on their own path; in my first commit (due 00:45 UTC) every stop but Mars, so your bytes do not change when it goes onto main. `--diag onemove` draws any stop on E without touching the list. MERCURY. The `at` that keeps your present height at T (0.0352 and 0.0370 radii): dive 0.763 (T in 758, now 744), rise 0.244 (T in 978, now 990). Under E the eye at your present T frames stands at 0.109 (744) and 0.121 (990) radii. The harness reads your worst step now as 0.233 and 0.243 of the mean, the speed at the place 0.815 in the rise (limit 0.08). ## The pace step (plan E in my own path), 22:31 to 23:10 sandbox clock

What it is. Each dive and each rise is one path from the far end (the hold going on at 690, the travel's own pose at 1035) to the terrain's own flight at 780 and 960. The change of view is only a frame on that path (dive 760, rise 977), picked where the eye stands at the old height of the change (0.035 and 0.036 radii).

- `plan.ts`: `cruise(time)` is the terrain's flight (the old exit term kept as `flight.lift` because it moves 957 to 959 by a few 1e-9 units and two pixels of 959 changed by one level without it); `low(time)` is `untwin(path)` outside the terrain span; `leg()` solves the largest rate of the log height from the two end rates and the whole fall; `swell()` is the closed integral of a rate that climbs in a line to its top and falls in a line, corners rounded by `round`; the same share drives the direction about the place, the attitude and the field of view; `settle` frames carry the terrain's own velocity over the seam. `rested(time)` is the spin's rest with a knee of 30 frames each side (744 and 990), exact inside 780 to 960 and from 1020 on, so the picture speed has no step where the rest starts and ends.
- `look.ts`: `flight.rest`, `flight.knee`, `flight.lift`; `path.dive { at 760, peak 0.5, round 0.1, settle 3 }`, `path.rise { at 977, peak 0.51, round 0.1, settle 12 }`; `glare.top` (the horizon dip, in radians, at which the surface glare is full: the old heights at the old change of view, so 780 to 959 keep their bytes); `halo` by frames is gone.
- `set.ts`: time knobs key on the new frames of the change of view; the spin calls `plan.rested`; the surface glare is `shine(time, side)`, one function of height on both sides of the change of view; the halo of the limb is a function of the log of the height between the change of view and the terrain's end (no frames).
