## Plan F step 3: the sphere under and round the relief in the close view, 23:12 to 23:50 sandbox clock

What it is. The close view drew only the relief meshes (ground and the wide mesh under it) on the limb plate and the sky, so wherever the frame looked past the edge of the meshes there was nothing. Now the planet itself stands under them: a second instance of the globe (`shell`), with the same plates, light and grade as the globe of the orbit view at the same moment, drawn in the close scene before the meshes.

- `set.ts`: `globeOf()` builds the two instances; `shell.mesh` sits in the `terrain` group after limb and rim (renderOrder -3), scale `closeRadius`, centre at `nearUp * -(closeRadius + 1)`, turn `toClose` (the anchor frame's inverse times the fit's globe turn), spin 0. `paint()` holds every per frame setter of the globe, so both instances are fed by one function. In the close view of a dive or a rise the set asks the film for the same moment drawn from the orbit side (`film.shotAt(frame, time, 'orbit')`), takes that side's light, Sun, glare Sun and line, turns them by `toLow` (close camera turn times the inverse of the orbit camera turn) and paints the shell with them.
- Presence is a function of height, not of the segment: `smoothstep(height, floor, floor + look.under.span)`, `floor` the larger of the terrain's own heights at its two ends (0.3017 close units over the camera's rest height), `span` 0.45. Full at and above 0.75 (frame 764 in the dive, 972 in the rise), nothing at and below the terrain's heights, so 780 to 959 keep their bytes and 779 to 780 and 959 to 960 show no step.
- The shell's silhouette is sunk by `look.under.sink` 0.283 close units (the largest limb sink 0.163 plus the wide mesh's own drop 0.12), so its limb stays under the meshes' horizon. Without it (sink 0.081, the dive's value) the sphere showed as a second contour 1 to 3 px above the meshes' horizon from 760 to 766, with dark dashes where the far edge of the wide mesh's matte lay on it.
- `globe.ts`: `uShow` (premultiplied presence, 1 for the orbit globe: same bytes) and `show()`; in `update` the centre is divided by the mesh scale so a scaled instance is still a unit sphere to the shader.
- `look.ts`: `under = { sink: 0.283, span: 0.45 }`.

Proof that the shell is in place: the same frame drawn both ways (`DIAG=beauty/side=orbit/noplace` against `DIAG=beauty/side=terrain`, 757, 760, 764, 771, folders s1o and s1t) shows the same ground at the same place, scale and turn.

## The finer level is a repeating function tile, not a unique plate (decided 00:34 on the 24th; this replaces the "plate of level 5" above)

- Why not a unique plate: G15 sets the full set at 1400 MB and Mercury stands at 1380 MB by the loader's count (six plates 537, wrap colour 179, wide colour 201, relief colour 169, relief alpha 42, sky terrain 151, rest 100). A 4096 RGBA plate with mips is 89.5 MB; level 5 for dive and rise is 179 MB, level 6 as much again. 20 MB are free. Nothing shown is given up and no limit is moved.
- What is built instead: `fine.png`, one channel, a repeating tile (period 2048 texels full, 1024 web, plus guard rows of 1/32 of the period above and below since the loader repeats only sideways). A function: small craters and rough ground, lit by the Sun of the place (7.03 deg high, toward east 0.794 north 0.595), mean 1. Recipe and numbers in `RECIPE.md`, script `assets-src/mercury/place/fine.py`, values in `plates.json` key `fine`.
- How it shows: the globe multiplies it on the ground of level 4 twice, at 2 times and 4 times the texels of level 4 (800 and 1600 texels per close unit; periods 2.56 and 1.28 close units, the second shifted by 0.37, 0.61 of a period). Each octave comes in by the texels a pixel of the level before it (`patches.fade`, 1.02 to 1.95), never by time. It shows only where no viewpoint saw the ground: outside the box of the close picture and outside the box of the wide picture (feather 0.03 of the box), so the relief meshes and their lay back stay as they were. In the plate's shadows (plate value under 0.05 to 0.22) its strength falls to 0.3, since a crater in shadow has no sunlit wall.
- Said plainly: the colour and the large forms in that corner are still level 4, magnified; the fine detail on top is a function that repeats every 2.56 close units (2.38 deg). It is not a second photograph of that ground.
- Memory: 2048 by 2176 one channel with mips, about 5.9 MB full, 1.5 MB web.
- Asked of handover_engineer (00:49, low priority, no answer needed): a proper `Relief.show(share)`; until then `set.ts` patches the Relief material with `uPresent` at build and throws if the shader strings are gone.

## Mail from belt_builder, about 00:58 sandbox clock on the 24th (verbatim): light.sunRadiusDeg 9.2 to 9.65

From belt_builder (far Sun), on the Builder's MAIL 430 point 4. Question for you as owner of src/stops/mercury/plan.ts: is light.sunRadiusDeg (9.2) read by anything but the drawn disc? The Builder's rule: if only the drawn body reads it, 9.65 is accepted and you commit it in your folder; if the light reads it (softness of shadows, light on the ground), the far Sun gets its own drawn radius in my config and the light keeps 9.2.
Why 9.65: at 615 sun.dir stands at (1837.5, 118.1), the approved image's disc at (1834.5, 120) is a circle of 385 px; a true sphere of 9.2 degrees there draws an ellipse of 405 by 364 px and its limb is inside the picture's by 8 px toward the frame centre, 18 px at the left, 29 px at the bottom. With 9.65 the limb is within 11 px along the whole visible arc.
What I found by reading main 65fb91d8 (please confirm or correct, you know your set):
1. src/director/film.ts 397: sunSwap = 1 / sin(sunRadiusDeg) of the stop after the Sun. This is the distance where the Sun travel ends (6.2546 now, 5.966 with 9.65), so the travel's path 450 to 500 changes: travel_builder's concern, and my wear span follows it.

3. src/space/sky.ts 1061 to 1067: the sky's own disc and glare, the small Sun law, and my far draw's size.
4. src/space/dust.ts 192: the disc in the dust when the glare is on.
5. src/stops/mercury/set.ts 309: soft = 0.6 * shot.sun.angularRadius for your clear and streak edges.
I found no reader in a ground or shadow shader. If your terrain or globe light reads the Sun's size by another road (a uniform of your own, sunplate.json's disc), say so. Because of reader 1 a change to 9.65 moves the travel; if you or the Builder prefer not to move it, I take the own drawn radius in my config. Tell me and the Builder which.
Also for you: the ride of the body in dive and rise is in my tree now by your formula (in the sky pass's world the body stands at skyTurn * A * sun.dir, A the inverse of film.sky.orbits of this stop in the dive and of the next stop in the rise, and it turns with it; weight 1 - shot.skyShare). ## 01:46 to 02:10 UTC, 24 Nov: the dive side difference at T, cause and cure

- Handover report on c6bbaf6b, draft: "mercury.dive side difference at T: FAIL 99th percentile of the side difference 8 over 4". Rise sides fine (mean 0.008, 99th 0). Where: the lower left corner of 760, the ground no viewpoint saw (level 4 and the fine tile), bounded by the edge of the close picture. With the tile off still 6 over 4, so not the tile.
- Measured, in this order:
  - The corner of the globe side is the corner of the terrain side moved by 0.68 px right and 0.17 px down (phase correlation on four windows, response 0.9 to 1.06). Above the edge of the close picture both sides are equal.
  - The plan in doubles (`out/work/mercury/place/tools/sidegap.ts`, `sidegap2.ts`): ground direction of both sides for seven pixels at T: gap 6e-8 rad, 0.003 px, dive and rise alike. So the poses are right.
  - The plate coordinate `g` written out as colour through the texels view on both sides (work tree only): globe against shell 0.05 and minus 0.52 texels of level 4 everywhere in the corner. The shell is where the uniforms say (0.01 texel), the globe is not.
  - The ray from the mesh is right on both sides (0.02 px). The body normal `n` and `n * uBodyToWorld` on the globe are right (0.004 of 1/4096). After `spinY(x, uSpin)` x is off by 2.1e-5 and z by 9.2e-5.
- Cause: `spinY` took `cos(uSpin)` and `sin(uSpin)` in the shader. On the renderer of this machine the cos of 0.0445 rad is off by about 1e-4. The globe turns by `uSpin`, the shell of the terrain side has spin 0 (cos 1 and sin 0 are exact), so at T the globe's ground sat 2e-5 rad (half a texel of level 4, 0.68 px in that corner) off the shell's. It was there before; the corner was soft then, so it stayed under the limit. With level 4 and the tile sharp in that corner it shows. The rise at T has no sharp ground of the globe outside the close picture, so it passed.
- Two cures measured, both at the cause, neither softens or covers:
  - First try: `globe.ts` takes the cos and sin from the plan in doubles (`uSpin` as a vec2), no trig of the spin in the shader. Sides: dive pass (mean 0.013, 99th 1, 99.9th 2, max 17), rise pass (mean 0.003, 99th 0, max 9). But every globe frame with a spin moves by the renderer's error, the hold too: draft, master, against `base`: 615 292 px differ, largest 4 levels; 689 20736 px, largest 3; 780, 870, 959 0 px. Against `m5`: 717 165599 px (largest 13), 742 1316435 (35), 757 395949 (112), 760 0, 978 6891 (1), 985 1302782 (84), 990 1282074 (40), 1035 13770 (7). The hold may not change (MAIL 213), so this is not taken; work folder `s6`.
  - Taken: both sides run the same arithmetic. The shell of the terrain side now gets the same `uSpin` as the globe, and its mesh is turned back by the same angle in doubles (`set.ts`: `shellBase`, `shellTurn`, `spun`). In exact arithmetic nothing moves; on this renderer the shell carries the same error as the globe, so the two sides meet. `globe.ts` is as on c6bbaf6b, so no globe frame changes a byte. Sides: "mercury.dive side difference at T: pass" (mean 0.004, median 0, 99th 0, 99.9th 1, max 26; 0.015 percent over 1, 0.002 percent over 4, 2 px over 16); "mercury.rise side difference at T: pass" (mean 0.003, 99th 0, 99.9th 1, max 9; 0.008 percent over 1, 0 px over 16).
  - What is given up with the taken cure: on this renderer the ground of the globe and of the shell sits 2e-5 rad (0.68 px at master in the nearest corner at T) off the relief meshes of the close view. That was so for the globe before; it is now so for the shell after T as well (761 to 779, 960 to 976), inside the feather of the close picture. Exact trig everywhere would end it but moves the kept hold frames by up to 4 levels: the Builder's call.
- Disk: the handover tool wants 3 GB free; cleared the old handover frames (731 MB), the PNGs of `f5` (sheets kept) and two plate saves in /tmp.
- Looks pushed 02:07 (`tmp/sides760b.png`, the fault and its cause).
- Proof of the taken cure on frames, draft, master, full (`out/work/mercury/place/s7`): against `base` 615, 689, 780, 870, 959 0 px each. Against `m5` (c6bbaf6b): 717, 742, 757, 779, 960, 965, 978, 985, 990, 1035 0 px each; 765 31 px by 1 level, 770 10 px by 1, 970 5 px by 1, 976 2 px by 1, 977 5 px by 1; 760 87837 px (largest 115, mean 0.25) and 761 60812 px (largest 105, mean 0.19), all in the bottom eighth of the frame left of the middle, the sharp ground outside the close picture, moved by the 0.68 px (crop `tmp/s7-760-crop.png`: the same craters, no new edge). 760 and 761 are the only frames where the shell shows much ground of its own.

### 03:38 UTC NOTE 501 from the Builder (to every stop builder and globe_engineer), verbatim
"A fault of the whole film, found by the first whole smooth report from the machines (Mercury, job 14): the same film frame at uhd is not the same picture as at 16 by 9 (one block differs by 45.8 levels at 713, limit 12).
CAUSE, found by mercury_builder: every globe brings its plate levels and its fine levels in by the texels of the parent a pixel of the format (uPatchFade 1.1 to 1.7 against gd * scale * size, gd from dFdx and dFdy). At uhd a pixel is half as large, so every level comes in at twice the height; at preview at half the height; in the live player it depends on the window. Brandon captures at uhd, so he gets another film than the one we judge. The term is in src/bodies/planet.ts (globe_engineer) and in the own globe code of mercury, venus, earth, jupiter, uranus, neptune and the Sun (src/stops/<stop>/globe.ts or planet.ts).
RULING (plan F: whatever blends does so by distance, height or angle): the measure is texels of the parent a 1080th of the frame height, an angle, not a pixel. In practice one factor, the height of the drawing buffer in pixels over 1080 (1 at 16 by 9, 2 at uhd, 0.5 at preview), multiplied into the density that a fade reads (parent, texels, fine, thin, cap, whatever your code calls it) and into nothing that only picks a mip for sampling.
PROOF, both by you before you name the commit: (a) at 16 by 9 the factor is 1 and every byte stays: the hashes of your own draft frames before and after are equal. (b) at uhd the picture becomes the 16 by 9 picture, only finer: npm run smooth -- --stop ID --only parity on your tree (seven frames), limits mean 12, coarse mean 3.2, one block 12, frame level 2.2.
THE SAME RULE holds for every other number in your stop that is counted in pixels and can be seen: the width of a feather, the radius of a glow, a threshold of a size on screen, a point size. If your parity is still red with the factor in, the panel names the block: find the term.
WHO: each stop builder, in the commit you name next (mercury_builder has it in work). globe_engineer: src/bodies/planet.ts and anything else under src/bodies and src/core that reads a density a pixel, as one commit on newest main (6884e80 or later), then ask me for the engine proof and say which stops draw with the shared code. saturn_coder and mars_place_builder: check your place code for the same kind of term and say what you found.
A red parity is a severe item in a stop's round."

### 03:51 UTC NOTE 504 from the Builder (to every stop builder), verbatim
"On NOTE 501 (the factor), two things from globe_engineer that save you a wrong factor:

And the edge limit (handover.place.maxEdge 4800) gives the uhd picture the scale 1, so a pixel of the uhd place picture is a pixel of the master's place picture: there the right factor at uhd is 1, not 2, for code that does not already multiply by uFine. Code that multiplies its density by uFine (the terrain shaders) needs the format's factor alone.
2. The shared helper, in proof now and on main as soon as it is clean (agent/engine-globe, globe_engineer names the commit): src/core/frame.ts with frameUniform (the format's height over the master's, set by the engine in applySize, usable as a uniform), setPass (the engine sets it while a place picture is drawn) and bufferFrame() (the two multiplied, for code that reads a density a pixel of the buffer and knows nothing of uFine).
RULING: use the helper, not a factor of your own. If your cure is already written with your own uniform (uranus_builder: seen.ts), keep working with it and swap its source to the helper when I tell you it is on main; that swap changes no byte and needs no new proof beyond the hashes. If you have not started, fetch globe_engineer's commit when it names it and write against the helper. Stops with no place picture left (plan F) have only case 1's last sentence to mind."

### 04:17 to 04:25 UTC the dark mark: its true cause and cure
- The lead direction changed nothing at the mark (the fill there already came from one direction, 165 deg; lead direction 11, share of the fill 0.993). Dump of the fields (`out/work/mercury/place/quad/dbg-sliver2.jpg`): the mark lies on the bisector of the inward corner of the rise's place (level 4 plate px 2256, 2203). Round that corner the outward direction turns from one side (124.2 deg) to the other (213.8 deg); more than about 37 deg from both, the two sides' weights sum to less than the floor 1e-6 that `spill()` divides by, so the copy read as having no source although both sides had one. The fill then put ground from 1.5 deg further in there, which is the black shadow of the large crater: a dark wedge with straight sides, 310 plate px long.
- Cure: `band.spill.fan.rise.leastSum` 1e-30 as the floor of the weight sum in the rise (the dive keeps 1e-6 and its bytes). The copy between the two outward directions is now the nearer side's copy. Level 4: px lacking a source 495556 to 253360, filled 242389, lead direction 11, share 0.99. Looked at the plate before and after at three places (`quad/sum-a.jpg`, `sum-c.jpg`, `sum-wide.jpg`): the quad, the dark wedge, the dark streak and the striped crater copy are gone; a faint hairline about 90 plate px long is left on the bisector.

### 04:51 UTC the pixel list (MAIL 366 point 3, NOTE 501, d193): what my folders count in buffer pixels
Read from the code while the whole smooth report runs. Scaled means multiplied by `uFrame` (`frameUniform`) so that it is an angle; at 16 by 9 the factor is 1.
1. `globe.ts` PATCH_LEVELS, FINE and the globe's own texels: the density that the fade between two levels reads. SCALED in this commit (the parity cure).
2. `globe.ts` main, the silhouette: `px = fwidth(s)`; the crest's taps are `uSil.z * px` apart (look.light.silhouette.taps 1.5, floor one texel of the height map); the edge is `px * uEdge` wide (look.light.edge 1.2 times the grad factor). NOT scaled. At 4K the crest is read over half the angle (never under one height texel) and the edge is 1.2 buffer px, half the angle. Seen at the limb of the hold and of the high dive and rise. Parity 615: mean 1.424, block -2.838, so it is inside the limits; it is the 16 by 9 limb, only finer. To do: scale the taps (a look), leave the edge (an antialias) unless the Builder rules otherwise.
3. `globe.ts` main, the smear of the final tier: `move` is the shift since the last sample in buffer px; `still = smoothstep(0.5, 1.0, sm)` and `smoothstep(2.0, 6.0, sm * uJitter.z)` read it as px. NOT scaled. Only with samples above 1 in the dive and rise (the parity part renders the draft tier and does not see it). At 4K a shift of half a 16 by 9 pixel already counts as moving. `sm * fd` (the fine level's fade under smear) is texels and holds at any format. To do: `sm / uFrame` in the two thresholds, proof on final tier frames at both formats.
4. `sunplate.ts smear()`: `px = angle * height / (2 tan)`, taps `ceil(px / step)` (at most 8), the mip widened by `soft * min(px, 4)`. NOT scaled; `height` is the buffer's. At 4K the taps reach 8 at half the turn and the widening stops at 4 buffer px, half the angle. Only with a camera before this one (final tier). To do: `px / bufferFrame()` for the widening, taps stay per buffer px (they are a sampling count, not a look).
5. `set.ts`: `radiusPx` from `view.format.height`; `globe.mesh.visible = radiusPx > 0.6` is 0.6 buffer px, so at 4K the globe is drawn down to half the angle. NOT scaled. Seen only far out on the legs, under the far point (`FarPoint`, cured on main by the globe engineer in d193, takes `radiusPx` in buffer px by its own rule). `holdRadius / (radiusPx / view.format.height)` is a share of the frame height. To do: `radiusPx / bufferFrame() > 0.6`.
6. `lens.ts`, `sunplate.ts` `dFdx(uv)`, the globe's `textureGrad` calls: they only pick a mip. Nothing to scale (NOTE 501).
7. `look.ts leaveStep`, `leaveSoft`: no reader left in my folder (rg finds only the two lines). Dead values, to remove.
8. Frame numbers on a pixel path: `flight.rest: [744, 990]`, `knee: 30`, `lift.at: 990` in the plan's params. They are times of the timeline, not pixels; listed because plan F asks that nothing comes in by time. They shape the path (the pace), not a blend. To check after NAMED that no weight of a picture reads them.
9. The terrain view (`src/terrain/relief.ts`, shared) is not in my folders; parity 870: mean 2.282, block -2.211.

### 04:52 UTC MAIL 526 from the Builder (to every stop builder and handover_engineer), verbatim
"MAIL 526 from the Builder (/root), 04:52 UTC, one mail to every stop builder and to handover_engineer: THE SEAM WITH THE SURFACE VIEW. A ruling (d196).

FOUND on Mars's rise at 60 pictures a second. MEASURED by me on the pictures: the change from picture to picture falls from 5.3 to 2.7 within three capture frames after the seam (film frame 2670) and is at 8.8 four later. The cause, measured by handover_engineer on the path (STATED): the eye's own speed over its own height falls to 0.33 of the surface view's at 2671 + 1/2 and is at 2.2 five capture frames later. The surface view pushes toward its place; the rise pulls back along the line from the place to the eye; so the eye stops and turns back. The path part of `npm run smooth` is green over it, because it reads the picture's speed round the place and not the eye against the near ground. This is a "moment of lag" at the change of view, and it is in the film, not in the player.

THE RULE. Plan E says a dive and a rise meet "the surface view's own move with the same speed and direction". That holds for the frames after the seam as well as at it: across each seam with the surface view the eye's speed over its own height never dips and never turns back. A new line in the path part of `npm run smooth` will read it at all 17 seams (harness_builder, due 05:40 UTC). Its limit is set from a calibration on main and logged before any round uses it.

WHERE IT IS CURED: in the surface view's own path, which is yours. Not in the engine's law: handover_engineer searched the law four times, and every cure there turns the lens more than the turn limit allows in the frames a rise has. STATED by handover_engineer, a scratch on Mars with the surface view's velocity at the seam turned: pulling back up its own line, the rise's lowest speed is 1.05 and it never turns back; across its lens 0.95 and 1.02; 45 degrees off 0.53; pushing at its place 0.33. A dive arrives down the surface view's way (0.98 and more on the one move).

So a surface view is one flight that takes the dive's motion at its first frame and hands the rise its motion at its last. Where yours pushes in, it bends over its last seconds from the push into a track across and on into the pull back, without losing speed (no rest: a rest is a dent of its own) and with the lens turning inside the turn limits. Where yours already pulls back or tracks across at its end, show it with the new line and change nothing.

WHAT MUST NOT CHANGE: the picture of the judged surface frame. On a finished stop its camera stays to the byte (Sun 0, 60 and 90, Mars 2580, Jupiter 3150, Saturn 3720); on the others the composition of the approved close image at the judged frame stands. The bend lives after the judged frame. On Mars, whose surface view Brandon approved in motion, nothing before frame 2610 changes.

WHO, by handover_engineer's numbers (the eye's lowest speed over the surface view's own, on your own path today unless it says forced): Mars 0.33 (rise), Uranus 0.46 at 4382 (rise), Earth 0.64 at 2116 (rise) and 0.69 at 1905 (dive), Venus 0.78 (dive), Mercury 0.83 at 961 (rise), Sun 0.92 (0.38 forced onto the one move), Jupiter 0.76 forced (its view is nearly still at the seam), Neptune 0.98, Saturn not measured. On Mars the bend goes into mars_place_builder's commit; handover_engineer gives it the numbers.

TIME. The 07:00 UTC point of d186 stands for everything else. A stop that needs this bend may have until 08:30 UTC for its NAMED mail if it tells me so in one line before 07:00 with what is done and what is left. I mail the sha of the new line as soon as it is on main; until then the engineer's scratch is the guide.

No answer is needed unless you ask for the later time or see a reason the rule cannot hold on your stop; then say which frame and why."
- For Mercury: 0.83 at 961 on the rise, by handover_engineer's numbers. To measure myself on my own path before I decide.

### 04:52 to 05:08 UTC: the seam measured on my own path, and the bend as a scratch (no change in src yet)
- Measure (`out/work/mercury/place/tools/seam.ts rise 950 975 0.5`; the eye in the body's own frame, speed over height against frame 959): 0.969 at 960, 0.898 at 960 + 1/2, 0.840 at 961, 0.817 at 961 + 1/2, 0.856 at 962, 0.977 at 962 + 1/2, 1.180 at 963. The motion's angle to the lens goes from 63.7 degrees at the end of the surface view (forward and up, 56 percent climb) to 167 degrees (the rise's pull back) within frames 960 to 966, up to 30 degrees a film frame at 962. So the rule is broken on the rise, as the Builder's mail says (0.83 at 961). The dive seam holds: 1.006 at 780, never under 1.0.
- The surface view's path today: `along = A (u + g u^3 / 3)`, `up = S u^2`, A 0.45, g 1, S 0.3, u = 2 (frame - 870.25) / 180, unit the eye's height at the judged frame; the lens is fixed, 30.05 degrees under the level. The rise takes position, speed and direction of the surface view at the seam (`leg()` reads `onGround(near +- e)`), so any end motion is met.
- Scratch (`out/work/mercury/place/bend/try2.ts`, `patch.ts`, `sm.ts`; they replace `Plan.prototype.cruise` in the tool's own process, nothing in src): the old path stays to frame `from`; then a straight lead along the heading it has there and a turn whose heading goes by a smoothstep of its length to the end heading (`end`, the angle to the lens). The two lengths are solved so that the seam pose stays where it is (position and lens: the rise's plates and the place stay valid). Timing: a cubic of the length that takes the old speed and gain at `from`. Joined to the old path by a smootherstep over `join` frames. A first try with one Hermite curve was rejected (a hairpin, 30 to 250 degrees a film frame).
- Read with from 876, join 12 (the judged frame 870 and every frame to 876 keep their path to the bit):

| end, degrees to the lens | lowest speed over height after the seam, against 959 | largest fall between half frames, 870 to 975 | largest turn of the motion, degrees a film frame | end speed against the old | lead, turn (eye heights) | furthest forward (old 0.600) | eye height against the old path, lowest |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 140 | 1.000 | 0 | 6.68 at 961 (5.88 at 950) | 1.46 | 0.40, 0.39 | 0.621 | 0.890 at 940 |
| 150 | 1.000 | 0 | 6.97 at 950 | 1.54 | 0.43, 0.38 | 0.639 | 0.887 at 941 |
| 160 | 1.000 | 0 | 8.04 at 950 | 1.63 | 0.46, 0.38 | 0.658 | 0.885 at 942 |
| 168 (the rise's own line) | 1.000 | 0 | 8.88 at 951 | 1.71 | 0.48, 0.39 | 0.675 | 0.885 at 942 |

- An end of 100 degrees still dips (0.985); 120 to 180 do not. A later start is worse (from 900: a fall of 0.0013 at 917). A fixed end speed by a quartic timing costs a dip of 0.3 to 1.2 percent at 959 + 1/2 unless the end speed is 1.4 times the old or more: the eye is 25 percent higher at the seam than where the turn starts, so the speed must grow through the turn to hold speed over height.
- `npm run smooth` path part on the patched plan (`BEND="from=876 end=140 join=12" npx tsx out/work/mercury/place/bend/sm.ts`, kind rise): green at every end angle. End 140: rate step 0.056 at 1005, turn 0.063 at 1008, speed 0.065 over 8 at 1030; at rate 2 0.057, 0.063, 0.066; seam steps 0.004 and 0.012.
- The lens does not turn in the surface view at all (fixed), so the turn limits of the lens hold by themselves; what turns is the motion.
- What the bend costs, to be read on frames: every frame from 877 on moves (the eye stays lower for longer, up to 11 percent nearer the ground at 940, and goes 2 to 7 percent of its height further forward before it comes back), and every frame of the rise moves (the rise starts 1.46 times as fast). So texels of the surface frames, the handover sides, pictures, parity and the pale quad frames are all read again after it.

### 05:57 to 06:12 UTC: the first bend made a pop in the surface view; the bend's shape changed
- The rise's report with the bend of 05:36 (end 165, `out/work/mercury/place/sm/mercury-rise-bend.md`): path green, level step 0.28, pattern 1.38 at 982 (74 frames over, as before the bend), and NEW: "RED pop score 1.28 from frame 954 to the next, limit 1.2 (4 frames over it, first 953)". Before the bend the same frames read 1.00.
- Cause, measured (`out/work/mercury/place/tools/popmap.py`, `/tmp/flowstat.py` on the report's own frames): no event in the picture. The measure works on a quarter size picture and carries the next frame back along the flow with a bilinear tap; what is left of fine ground detail grows with the fractional part of the flow (1.4 at a fraction under 0.1, 2.9, 3.7, 4.6, 5.5 at 0.4 to 0.5). At 953 + 1/2 to 955 the eye of the first bend rose almost straight up, so the whole ground moved the same way by about half a quarter pixel a capture frame: 64 to 87 percent of the ground had a fraction of 0.4 or more (19 to 24 percent in the frames round it), the left over rose from 3.2 to 4.1 for four frames and read as a pop.
- A model of it without rendering (`out/work/mercury/place/bend/popmodel.py`: the ground as a plane one height under the judged eye, the flow of every quarter pixel, the left over from the table above, the pop as the tool takes it): 1.175 for the first bend where the tool measured 1.28, same frame (954).
- Scans on the model and on the plan (`bend/scan.py`, `scan3.py`, `/tmp/eyeside.py`): a straight end after the turn only sharpens the turn (12 to 50 degrees a frame); a single arc without a lead cannot reach the seam (the seam lies 27.6 degrees up from 876 and the way must end going back). What helps: a lower end angle (the eye still rises more than it backs away at the seam, the rise turns the rest: its way is 128 to 134 degrees at 964 for every end angle) and a turn that is quick early and slow at its end. `flight.bend.skew`: the heading runs on 1 - (1 - q)^skew before its smoothstep.
- Chosen: `flight.bend = { from: 876, join: 12, endDeg: 140, skew: 1.7, steps: 64 }`. On the plan: main's seam tool rise "the surface view flies 0.01450 of its height a film frame | lowest 1.025 at 960 | never against the surface view's way | surface view, largest step a film frame 0.015 at 939 + 1/2 | change of view at 977", dive unchanged ("lowest 1.014 at 779 + 1/2"); the surface view's own 16 frames before the seam never under 0.933 of the seam's (limit 0.87); the way of the eye turns by at most 9.4 degrees a frame (946 + 1/4) in the surface view and 6.5 (960 + 3/4) in the rise; path part of smooth green (rise: rate 0.057 at 1006 + 1/2, turn 0.063 at 1008, speed 0.066 over 8 at 1030 + 1/2); eye positions bit equal for the eighth frames 780 to 876, first that differs 876 + 1/8, seam point apart 0.
- Measured on rendered frames (draft, rate 2, frames 944 to 964, `out/work/mercury/place/b2`, `tools/popk.py` which calls the tool's own `pictures`): largest pop 1.067 at 955 (limit 1.2); the same script on the first bend's frames reads 1.274 where the report had 1.28.

### 06:49 UTC: mail from handover_engineer (verbatim, low priority)
"handover_engineer, 06:49 UTC. Your ask of last night (a share on the relief so your patch of its shader can go) is built. WHAT: `Relief.presence(share)` in src/terrain/relief.ts (the name `show()` is the shield's). It multiplies the premultiplied output of every layer and of the air by the share, the same expression as your `vec4(col * alpha, alpha) * uPresent`. Off until a set calls it. A share outside 0 to 1 throws. Under 1 the relief is no shield (screen answers false).

Your own patch must go in the same commit, else the share lands twice.
